# The Linux build

Zexion's Linux binary (`builds.md`, "B1 on Linux") against what `memory.md` records for the
Windows exe. The Linux binary keeps its symbol table and loads at fixed addresses, so a global
is found by its name and a field by its offset from that name; the Windows exe has neither, so
its targets were found by byte pattern. Every address on this page is a link address of the
Linux binary, which is where it is at run time (it is `ET_EXEC`; seen in the game: "Read from
the running game").

Every target of `client/patterns.py` is mapped. Most facts here are from the binary on disk;
"Read from the running game" records what has been read from the game itself on 2026-11-18 (through
the Linux helper, `native/README.md`), "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19" what the owner saw on
the 19th, and "Reading the running game" is from the test PC and a stand-in
game. The disassembly is GNU `objdump -d -C
-M intel` of a copy in `lab/linux-b1/` (`all.asm`), which names each rip-relative operand as
`symbol+offset`.

## Lookup: targets

Each Windows target in `client/patterns.py`, where it is on Linux, and how sure (the "How" of a
row naming a section: the evidence is that section's row for it). "Shift" means the Windows
address less `0x3CF6A0`, plus `gGameState`'s address ("`gGameState` has the Windows layout");
"refs" counts the instructions in `all.asm` whose operand is exactly that `gGameState+offset`.
`tests/test_patterns.py` fails while a target has no row here.

| Target | Windows (B1) | Linux (B1) | How | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Every target in `client/patterns.py` now has a Linux counterpart: the first rows from `LoadGame`,
the live reads and the shift (the sections below the Lookup), the rest by group (the first four
"Mapped by disassembly" sections; the fifth maps the item looks' fields, not targets, the sixth
what the traps write and patch, the seventh the map's layout the cursor's names read, the eighth
what the helper's translated text relies on beyond the translations' targets, the ninth what the
disks' count relies on beyond the menus' targets, the tenth what the RETURN HOME prompt relies on,
where `fade` is measured, the eleventh what received scrap's drops rely on beyond the events'
targets, the twelfth what a received Energy Shield relies on beyond the drops: the player's link
to it, its pool and handle, which the client's check reads, the thirteenth Intel Chips' record,
the fourteenth what the ASSIST lock relies on beyond the menus' targets, the fifteenth what the
title screen's lines rely on beyond the menus' targets: the title's list, its line and its
clearing, the sixteenth what the Feats guard's Steam stand-in and load test rely on: the Steam
accessors and their threads, the load, and the save slots' arrays, where `slot_array` and
`rando_slots` are measured, the seventeenth what the Feats of Natu screen's note relies on:
MISSION COMPLETE and the way on, the screen's menu, title and hints, and the achievements it sets,
the eighteenth what the file select's AP logo and disk counts rely on: the entries' fill, the
fills outside their handlers, the empty font cells, and a new game's and a load's fades, and the
nineteenth what the helper's fill of the major-item list relies on: the pickup's append, every
reader and writer of the list, the ITEMS row, the records' icons and the new game's start-with
array). Functions and globals are named symbols; the code points the helper patches sit in GCC's
unoptimized code, so their bytes and patch shapes differ from the Windows ones, and each section's
"What differs" says how.

## Mapped by disassembly: HUD, items, sound, popups, deaths

Each Windows target of this group, the Linux code or data that does what `memory.md` describes for
it, and how that was shown. Addresses are the Linux binary's; "measured" here means by
disassembly: the Linux code was read doing what `memory.md` records, with the same arguments,
fields and callers. None was read in the running game except where the Lookup says so and "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19" lists.

| Target | Windows | Linux | How found | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `message_fn` | `+0x182670` | `0x631C5B` `createGameMessage(char const*, int, int, int, int, int, int)` | The pickup's item branch in `playerCollideProc` calls it with (text, 24, 160, 88, 100, 1, 180): `0x642AAF`–`0x642AB7` (icon path) and `0x642D8B`–`0x642D93` (no icon). memory.md has per_line 24, x 160, y 88, frames 180, with the 5th and 6th never read on Windows. **On Linux they are read** (by disassembly, 2026-11-19): it stores them at the message's `+0x1F4` and `+0x1F8` (`0x631F81`–`0x631F91`), and `GameMessageMoveProc` (`0x631445`) shrinks the text window's half-width `+0x214` and half-height `+0x218` by them each frame as it closes (`0x631521`–`0x631556`) and grows them as it opens (`0x631671`–`0x6316A6`), through `setGameMessageStringClipping`: the steps of the box's opening and closing. With 0 and 0 (the helper's call until then) the popup popped in and out whole, its opening and closing frames showing nothing (seen in the game, 2026-11-19, B1 on Linux, Flatpak Steam: an Energy Tank's popup); the helper now passes the game's 100 and 1 (`kPopupStepX`, `kPopupStepY` in `src/text.h`), and a Missile Container's popup opened and closed as the game's own do (the same day, the helper `b98752ac…`). Inside it: it reads the last message `gGameState+0x13350`, checks it with `verifyIndexedSpritePtr` (`[p+0x1D8]` against the sequence number at `gGameState+0x13358`, `0x4D31BC`), and sets that message's frames left `+0x1EC` to 1 (`0x631CB9`). It looks for a free slot among 4 slots of `0x1628` bytes (busy word at `+0x1D8`, `0x631D14`–`0x631D22`) and returns 0 when all are busy (`0x631E05`). It numbers the new slot from `gGameState+0x1CD4C` into `+0x1D8` (`0x631E39`–`0x631E52`), sets half-height `+0x1FC`, calls the window function `setGameMessageStringClipping` (`0x63216B`), and stores itself as the last message (`0x63219D`, `AttachIndexedSprite`). | measured (by disassembly) | SysV: the 7th argument goes on the stack (`push 0xb4`); the helper's `MessageFn` typedef works unchanged. An equivalent 14-byte patch is `8B 45 E4` (mov eax,[rbp-0x1c]) `2B 45 F4` (sub eax,[rbp-0xc]) `83 E0 07` (and eax,7) `0F 1F 44 00 00` (5-byte nop). `ecx` is set from `eax` after it and `edx` is reloaded at `0x56962B`. The helper builds this patch on Linux (`src/text.cpp`, `text_cut_patch`'s Linux branch). It is right because the top cut is taken only while the edge is past the row's top and within its 7 (`56954d: mov eax,[rbp-0xc]` / `add eax,7` / `cmp [rbp-0x1c],eax` / `jg` past it, then `cmp eax,[rbp-0x1c]` / `jge` past it), so the edge less 10r is the cut, 1 to 7, and `[rbp-0xc]` is written once a glyph (`0x5690DE`, its only store in the function). There is also a **second top-cut branch** that Windows's description lacks: when the glyph's `+0x38` == `0x5A` it uses draw proc 27 and top mod **9** (`0x5695AA`–`0x5695E7`, `imul 0x38e38e39`). The patch above leaves that branch alone. The Windows patch's byte check is MSVC-specific and refuses this code, so the Linux one checks these 14 bytes. |

The game calls `_Znwm` (operator new) 0 times and `malloc@plt` 71 times. The menu items come from `malloc` (`createGameMenuItem`, `0x56BD7B`). The GOT is lazily bound (no `BIND_NOW`; the RELRO segment ends at `0x6BB000`). |

With kind 0 it calls `gameControllerRumble` twice (memory.md: the shake routine calls `SDL_GameControllerRumble`) and counts the death at `gGameState+0xAD4` (the shift of Windows `+0x3D0174`, `0x51C23E`). Unless the save byte `+0x935E` is 4 or 5 it stops the music and sounds (`0x51C26B`–`0x51C29C`), then later posts a popup (`0x51C8E9`, `0x51D036`). | measured (by disassembly) | The player argument is the same object as `gMainCharacterSpriteP`: it reads `+0xA4C` and `+0x1100` from it. The kind is in `esi`, matching the helper's `DeathFn(void*, char)` under SysV. **The death's popup, by disassembly (2026-11-18):** both posts (`0x51C8E9`, `0x51D036`) pass `createGameMessage`'s return unchecked to `SWSetSpriteIndexedMoveProc(message, gMoveProcs, -1)` (`0x51C909`, `0x51D056`), which with −1 writes `[message+0x178]` = 0 at once (`0x4CC664`); `createGameMessage` returns 0 only when no slot of its four is free, after setting its "ERROR-TOO GAME MESSAGES" line (`0x631D5F` → `0x631E05`; the text at `0x6954FB`; its two other loads of 0, `0x63201B` and `0x632083`, are negations for `SWSetSpriteRect`, not returns). So a death with every message slot busy would store through a null message (inferred from the code, not run), as on Windows (`0x1DA6EF`): the helper's `kill` refuses while a message shows (`refused message showing`), on both builds. |
| `halt_flag` | `+0x345EF4` | `0x12B456E` `gEditorMode` (1 byte) | `PlayerDestruction` returns at once while it is set (`0x51C179`). Its writers match memory.md's: toggled by `ToggleEditorMode` (`0x4E1C47`, Windows "toggled at `0x268B4`"), set to 1 in `init()` (`0x66DEE5`, Windows "set to 1 at start-up, `0x78BFC`, beside the menu state", here the instruction just before `gCursorMode`'s store), and cleared in `gameEventLoop` (`0x671A76`, Windows `0x7C672`). It has 89 references. | measured (by disassembly) | A fourth writer exists, `EditorUpdateKeys` storing 0 (`0x4E270C`), which memory.md does not list. |
| `menu_state` | `+0x345F03` | `0x12F9901` `gCursorMode` (1 byte) | `PauseGame()` sets it exactly as memory.md describes the file-select or options builders: 2 when it reads 1 or 2, else a call through the first item's `+0x2C8` with 3 (`0x618D28`–`0x618D6F`), and about 30 menu builders do the same. `init()` stores 0 next to `gEditorMode` = 1 (`0x66DEEC`). `exitMenuCount(int)` stores 1, 0 and 2 (`0x559AA4`, `0x559B38`, `0x559E5E`: "1 for a moment while one closes"). The highlight procs store 3. | measured (by disassembly: the writers and the menu builders' test, as `memory.md` describes); read live on Linux (2026-11-19, B1 on Linux, Flatpak Steam on the test PC, `tools/grant.py watch` through the Linux helper while the owner used the mouse): 0 in play with the mouse moved, non-zero while the pause menu was open, and 0 again on its closing, by the keyboard or by a mouse click after the mouse was used in it; on Windows not read | **Caveat:** the name says what it is, the mouse/menu cursor mode. `handleMouseModeActivationCheck()` sets it from 0 to 1 when the mouse moves or clicks (`0x4E0AC8`–`0x4E0B03`), and `handleMouseGameModeDeactivationCheck()` sets it back to 0. So in play with the mouse in use it may read 1, which the helper's "in play" test (`menu_state == 0`) would take as a menu. In play it did not (the game tracks the mouse only in menus): the read is in this row's Status, above. |

### What differs from Windows

- **The shift holds well beyond the four checked fields.** These Linux reads land exactly at the shifted Windows addresses:
  - the item pool (`+0x79E990`) and the object pool (`+0x388DA0`) with their next-free counters;
  - the message slots (`+0x8C6B48`);
  - the death count (`+0xAD4`);
  - the second per-cell map byte (`+0x15B69`, Windows `+0x3E5209`, in the pickup at `0x642960` and in `createUpgrade`);
  - the color slot table (`+0x137FF`, Windows `+0x3E2E9F`);
  - `fade_level` (`+0x138A3`, read by `setSpritePalette`);
  - `frame_clock` (`+0xB0`, read by the item spawn and zeroed in `init()` next to the `save_loaded` stores);
  - `last_message` (`+0x13350`, with the sequence number at `+0x13358` compared to `[p+0x1D8]` by `verifyIndexedSpritePtr`).

  The Lookup records `last_message` and `frame_clock` as measured on this evidence. `gGameState` holds no `wchar_t` or `long` fields up to at least `+0x8C6B48`. The pools are reached by `lea reg,[rax+disp]` plus `gGameState`, so `objdump` names no symbol for them: search for the raw displacement as well.
- **The structure sizes and offsets match MSVC's:** SpriteRec `0x1D8` (the template copy), item object `0x3B0`, general object `0x5C8`, message slot `0x1628`, item record 100 bytes. These fields are at the same offsets: sprite `+0x198`, `+0x1A0`, `+0x1A8`, `+0x1B0`, `+0x1BC`, `+0x1C4` and `+0x1D8`; message `+0x1EC`, `+0x1FC`, `+0x200`, `+0x204`, `+0x214`, `+0x218` and `+0x220`; glyph cut `+0xD8` to `+0xF0`; player `+0xA4C`, `+0xB30`, `+0xBB8`, `+0xCBC` to `+0xCC2`, `+0xCEA`, `+0xCEC`, `+0x103C`, `+0x108E`, `+0x10D8`, `+0x1100` and `+0x1108`. The item looks' general-object and other sprite fields (`+0x30`, `+0x40`, `+0x48`, `+0x120`, `+0x1D0`; `+0x1E8`, `+0x210`, `+0x211`, `+0x32C`, `+0x32E`) also match: "Mapped by disassembly: item looks".
- **The globals outside `gGameState` are simply named symbols** (`gPowerUpDefs`, `gSpriteWorldP`, `gMasterSpritesP`, `gSpriteDrawProcs`, `gGameSoundManager`, `gEditorMode`, `gCursorMode`, `gPreferences`). None is at a shifted address.
- **ABI and code differences the Linux helper handles:**
  - `updateNumberDisplay`'s third bool: pass 1.
  - `popup_width_code`'s opcode `0xBE`.
  - `text_cut_code`, which must be rebuilt for the -O0 stack-local code (done: `src/text.cpp`). It has 14 bytes, and the mod-9 branch is extra.
  - No allocator slots are needed.
  - The weapon-strip gate is a preference that defaults to 1.
- `menu_state` is the cursor mode, which the mouse can set to 1, but only in menus: read live in play, it stays 0 (2026-11-19).
- All the code is unoptimized GCC (frame pointer, locals in `[rbp-…]`). Calls are direct `call rel32` to named functions, so these function targets need no byte patterns: the binary is `ET_EXEC` and keeps its symbols.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18), each row as its Status says; run in the game in part, 2026-11-19 ("Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19": received items' popups, a death dealt by DeathLink, tall popups' text; the popups' sounds and the HUD after the grants not checked); open: `text_cut_code` (the mod-9 branch, not seen); `menu_state` read live in play, 2026-11-19 (its row) |
| Method | `memory.md`'s description of each Windows target matched against `all.asm` (the Linux disassembly), the symbol table (`nm -C`, `nm -S`), the relocations (`readelf -r`) and, for initialized data, `client/elf.py`'s reads of the binary; four parallel reviews, each spot-checked against `all.asm` before it was recorded |
| Build | B1 (Linux), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-18, at the addresses each row cites; `text_cut_code`'s guard (`0x56954D`–`0x569562`) and the one store of `[rbp-0xc]` (`0x5690DE`) re-read there, and its patch run in `native/linux/tests/patch_tests.cpp`, 2026-11-19. Re-checked by hand: `popup_width_code` (`be 18 00 00 00` at `0x642D8B`, `objdump`), `item_pool` (`lea rdx,[rax+0x79e990]` at `0x48E205` and `0x4AD97D`), `weapon_strip_gate` (`ClearPreferences()` storing 1 at `0x4C28DA`). The docs review the same day re-checked about 60 of the page's facts against `nm -S`, `readelf` and `all.asm` (symbol addresses and sizes, the GOT and PLT slots, the patch sites, the reference counts) and found them all as recorded; the other rows are as the review agents read them. |

Each Windows target of this group, the Linux code or data that does what `memory.md` describes for
it, and how that was shown. Addresses are the Linux binary's; "measured" here means by
disassembly: the Linux code was read doing what `memory.md` records, with the same arguments,
fields and callers. None was read in the running game except where the Lookup says so and "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19" lists.

### What differs from Windows

- **Struct offsets agree** wherever checked: widget (`+0x1D8` panel, `+0x1E0` index, `+0x1E8` rect, `+0x1F0` label, `+0x1F8` record, `+0x230` code/slot, `+0x2C0` callback, `+0x2C8` handler), panel (`+0x1E0` children, `+0x1E8` capacity, `+0x1EC` selectable, `+0x1F0`/`+0x1F8` sprites, `+0x200`/`+0x260` frames, `+0x278` pieces, `+0x2B8` width, `+0x2DC` selected, `+0x2E4` index, `+0x2F8` handler), menu (`+0`, `+8`, `+0x20`, `+0x68`, `+0x70`, `+0x78`, size `0x80`), text (`+0x1D8`, `+0x1DC`, `+0x1E0`, `+0x1E4`, `+0x1E8`, `+0x1EC`, `+0x228`, `+0x230`, `+0x238`, `+0x248`, size `0x268`), sprite (`+0x30`, `+0x98`–`+0xD0`, `+0x148`, `+0x1A8`, `+0x1BC`, `+0x1C4`), layer (`+0x18`/`+0x20`) and the randomizer menu block. So nothing before those offsets changes size between the ABIs. Fields past them were not checked.
- **Calling convention:** every function the helper calls (`createGameMenuItem`, `moveGameMenuItem`, `moveSpriteString`, `SWChangeMasterSprite`, `addGameMenu`, `alterSpriteString`, `newSpriteString`, `addSpriteString`) is SysV; `createGameMenuItem` puts its last three arguments on the stack where Windows puts five. Typed C++ function pointers compiled for Linux handle it; any hand-written thunk must not assume the MS layout.
- **Callbacks take no arguments on Linux** (`void(*)()`, called bare by `StandardMenuAction`), while the helper's Windows wrappers are typed `(widget, event)`; wrappers in the callback slot must not use them. Handlers (panel and widget) do get `(GameMenuItem*, int)` in `rdi`, `esi`.
- **Code patches need new bytes:** `keys_branch` is `75 3C` at `0x618A7E` (Windows `75 2E`). The other group-B targets are function entries and data, so no patch bytes.
- **Two divergences from memory.md:** the equipment screen's panels 5 and 6 have other handlers on Linux; `text_set_fn` has an identical twin. Neither affects how the helper uses them.
- **Fixed addresses:** all of these are link addresses in an ET_EXEC binary, so the Linux helper can take them from the symbol table (`nm`) rather than byte patterns; the code bytes quoted above are from the B1 copy only.

|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18), each row as its Status says; no part of it looked at on its own in the game; the parts that use these targets ran, 2026-11-19 (the game-mode panel's ARCHIPELAGO choice, the title screen's lines, the pause screen's disk row: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19"); no row open |
| Method | `memory.md`'s description of each Windows target matched against `all.asm` (the Linux disassembly), the symbol table (`nm -C`, `nm -S`), the relocations (`readelf -r`) and, for initialized data, `client/elf.py`'s reads of the binary; four parallel reviews, each spot-checked against `all.asm` before it was recorded |
| Build | B1 (Linux), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-18, at the addresses each row cites. Re-checked by hand: `keys_branch` (`PauseGame()` testing `gGameState+0x114` at `0x618A75`, `75 3c` at `0x618A7E`), `current_menu` (`gLastGameMenuP`, 340 references, written at `0x56A72E` as a new menu goes on top). The docs review the same day re-checked about 60 of the page's facts against `nm -S`, `readelf` and `all.asm` (symbol addresses and sizes, the GOT and PLT slots, the patch sites, the reference counts) and found them all as recorded; the other rows are as the review agents read them. |

| `context_init_slot` | `+0x209C98` (IAT slot) | GOT slot `0x6BB5C8` (`.got.plt`, `R_X86_64_JUMP_SLOT SteamInternal_ContextInit`), reached through the PLT entry `0x40BEB0` (`.plt.sec`: `endbr64; bnd jmp [rip+0x2af70d]`) | `readelf -r`; `all.asm` `0x40BEB0`. Callers: `SteamUserStats()` `0x63C093` and `SteamUtils()` `0x4C5E57` only | measured (by disassembly: the slot, its relocation, its lazy binding, its two callers); assumed (the two ways to redirect it in the notes, and the lazy resolver overwriting a hooked slot: reasoning, not tried then; route 2 built and run on a stand-in game, `tests/test_native_linux.py`, 2026-11-19; the guard it serves ran in the game at an ending, with no Steam achievement shown, run 2, 2026-11-19: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19") | `libsteam_api.so` is `NEEDED`; there is no `BIND_NOW`/`FLAGS` entry and `GNU_RELRO` ends at `0x6BB000`, so `.got.plt` is lazily bound and stays writable. Until the first call the slot holds `0x40AB90` (the lazy stub `push 0xb6; jmp .plt`). Two ways to redirect: (1) write the slot, as on Windows, with a stub that forwards other contexts to the real function obtained by `dlsym(RTLD_DEFAULT or RTLD_NEXT, "SteamInternal_ContextInit")`, never to the slot's old value (if not yet bound, calling the lazy stub makes the resolver overwrite the slot and undo the hook); (2) since the helper is in `LD_PRELOAD`, export `SteamInternal_ContextInit` from it and forward with `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT, ...)`, as for `SDL_GL_SwapWindow`: the dynamic linker then binds the game's slot to the helper's definition, with no write (but `exports.map` must list it, and it holds for the whole process). The stub's context argument is `rdi` (SysV), not `rcx`. |

### What differs from Windows

- **The Linux build is unoptimized** (GCC 9.4, `rbp` frames, every value reloaded from memory, nothing inlined). Every code-patch target must be re-derived: the Windows byte checks and patch shapes (the 50-byte seen-marker rewrite, the `FF 15` + `mov rdx` ending check, `cmp cx, r12w` in the ailment tests) do not match. The upside: no live registers across the patch sites (only stack locals), so jumps over stretches are easier to check.
- **Steam calls go through game wrappers.** All 23 achievement sets go through `setTheAchievement(int)` `0x5459E3` (and clears through `clearTheAchievement(int)` `0x545A38`), each calling `SteamUserStats()` (not inlined). So hooking `setTheAchievement`/`clearTheAchievement`, or the one accessor, is an alternative to the GOT slot on Linux.
- **Several Windows globals map onto named Linux globals outside `gGameState`**, with layouts that match where checked: `gExitActionP`/`gExitDelay`/`gExitActions`, `gAreaEvents`/`gAreaEventCount`/`gAreaEventProcs`, `gMoveProcs` (entry 120), `gSmallTexts`, the `rStartingLocation*` arrays, `gRandoPreferences` (= `randomizer_menu`), `gGameUnlocks` (= Windows `+0xCDFC80`: Feat bytes at `+0x43E`, `+0xCE00A8` at `+0x428`). Not by a fixed shift: by name and code.
- **Side corroborations of the other groups' targets:** `forceSeed`'s `[gLastGameMenuP]+0x20` matches Windows' `current_menu` (`+0xDF82F8`) use, as the menus section finds `current_menu` to be `gLastGameMenuP` `0x12F5700`. `EndingCutscene` sets flag 251 at `gGameState+0x1E8` and flag 250 at `+0x1E7`, which agrees with `flags` = `gGameState+0xED` by the shift. `weaponCool`'s tests agree with `ailments` = `gGameState+0x13724`.
- **ABI.** Handlers and game functions take SysV arguments (`rdi`, `rsi`, `rdx`, …): the area-event handlers `(char row, char col, AreaEvent*)`, `forceSeed()` and `generateRando()` take none. `wchar_t`/`long` play no part in any group-C struct: the area events, Text entries, start table, section and Steam context are byte, short, pointer or file-defined.
- **Not in memory.md:** `weaponCool` has a third timer under the same AILMENTS rule, player `+0xDA0` (`0x5081C2`..`0x5081FB`: 3 a frame at 1, zeroed at 2). Whether the Windows build has it too is unknown; the trap patches don't need it.

|---|---|

| Method | `memory.md`'s description of each Windows target matched against `all.asm` (the Linux disassembly), the symbol table (`nm -C`, `nm -S`), the relocations (`readelf -r`) and, for initialized data, `client/elf.py`'s reads of the binary; four parallel reviews, each spot-checked against `all.asm` before it was recorded |

Re-checked by hand: `ailments_hit_code` (`movzx eax,[gGameState+0x13724]`, `cmp ax,0x1`, `75 1b` at `0x507FCB` to `0x507FD6`), `context_init_slot` (`readelf -r`: `R_X86_64_JUMP_SLOT SteamInternal_ContextInit` at `0x6BB5C8`; RELRO ending below it; no `BIND_NOW`). The docs review the same day re-checked about 60 of the page's facts against `nm -S`, `readelf` and `all.asm` (symbol addresses and sizes, the GOT and PLT slots, the patch sites, the reference counts) and found them all as recorded; the other rows are as the review agents read them. |

Args 5 and 6 ×2 go to the HD object's `+0x1E4` and `+0x1E8` (`0x6380A6`, `0x6380B6`). Arg 7 goes to `+0x1DC` and `+0x1E0` (`0x638161`, `0x63816E`). The game passes the popup's own values (24, 160, 88, 100, 1, 180). The helper passes 0 for these on Windows, which would be wrong on Linux; on Linux it passes the pickup's (`PopupAreas::show`). Whether Windows uses them too: not checked. |
| `hd_build_fn` | `+0x88480` | `0x54DA05`, `generateText(float, float, std::wstring, GameFont*, SpriteString*, bool)` | Called from `createTranslationGameMessage` with x and y (the message sprite's `+0x40`/`+0x48`), a `std::wstring`, the font `gCurrentTranslation+0xD0`, the HD object's `+0x230` label, and 0 (`0x6382E5`–`0x638323`). Hides the label's old glyphs: `+0x238` array over `+0x240` (`0x54DCB7`–`0x54DD22`). Stores x and y as doubles at `+0x258`/`+0x260` (`0x54DD33`, `0x54DD4A`) and `+0x24C` = 1 (`0x54DD6D`), counting lines. At the end: count `+0x248`, shown `+0x244`, built byte `+0x230` = 1 (`0x550C30`–`0x550C57`). Tests LF, CR, tab, `{` and `}`. 68 call sites. | measured (by disassembly) | Same label offsets as Windows. **Calling convention and ownership differ.** x and y in `xmm0`/`xmm1`; `rdi` = pointer to the caller's `std::wstring` temporary (passed by value through a hidden reference); `rsi` = font; `rdx` = label; `ecx` = flag. Under the Itanium ABI **the caller destroys the string** after the call (`~basic_string` `0x492556` at `0x63832F`). Windows' callee frees it (`jmp 0x17D10`). |
| `wstring_new_fn` | `+0x17BD0` | `0x492582`, `std::__cxx11::wstring::basic_string<std::allocator<wchar_t>>(wchar_t const*, std::allocator<wchar_t> const&)` (weak); destructor `0x492556` | What every `generateText` caller seen uses to build its argument: `createTranslationGameMessage` `0x6382E0` (then `~basic_string` `0x63832F`) and `resetTranslationMessage` `0x6392E0`. 74 call sites. | measured (by disassembly) as the role; **not the same function** | Not an assign of (pointer, length) into a zeroed object. It is a constructor: `rdi` = 32 bytes of uninitialized storage, `rsi` = NUL-terminated 4-byte `wchar_t*`, `rdx` = an allocator reference (empty type, address unused). There is also `assign(wchar_t const*)` `0x63DFC0` (1 call). The layout is libstdc++'s (pointer `+0`, length `+8`, local buffer `+0x10` holding 3 characters and a NUL), not MSVC's (buffer or pointer `+0`, size `+0x10`, capacity `+0x18`, 7 characters inline). libstdc++ is linked dynamically (`NEEDED libstdc++.so.6`), so a GCC-built helper could build and destroy its own `std::__cxx11::wstring` on the same heap instead of calling these. |

### Where the translation layouts differ from Windows

- **Characters are 4 bytes everywhere.** `loadTextFile` widens each UTF-16 unit of a translation file to a 4-byte `wchar_t`, with no surrogate decoding. So the SmallText, LargeText, ItemNames, Database, Config and Controls buffers hold 4-byte characters, and every offset table counts 4-byte characters (`setupTextList`'s `[rax*4]`). The entry and offset values match Windows for the same file. Byte addresses double: text + offset×4. Room per entry, in characters, is unchanged, but the bytes double. The icon characters are 32-bit: `U+E024`+ is `byte + 0xDF64` (`0x642BEA`), with `0x6E` giving `0xE04F` and `0x77` giving `0xE050`. The parser cuts at `----` as on Windows.
- **What the helper's `translation.cpp`/`.h` needs on Linux, and has:** `sizeof(wchar_t)` reads and writes (`read_text`, `write_text`, `writable(... * sizeof(wchar_t))`), correct as the helper is built with GCC's 4-byte `wchar_t`; no code there assumes 2-byte units. `tr::kSize` is `0x40150` on Linux, not `0x40138`. Struct offsets `+0x00`–`+0xD0`, the `hd::` and `message::` namespaces and the `hdlabel::` offsets the helper uses are all unchanged. `FontMetrics`' glyph records are not: `0x40` bytes with 64-bit fields, which it reads on Linux ("Mapped by disassembly: the helper's translated text").
- **Strings passed to the game:** `generateText` takes a libstdc++ `std::__cxx11::wstring` by hidden reference, and the caller must destroy it. Windows' `hd_build_fn` takes an MSVC `std::wstring` and frees it itself. The helper's Windows pattern (build with `wstring_new_fn` on 32 zero bytes, then hand over) does not carry over. On Linux: construct (`0x492582`, or the helper's own libstdc++ `wstring`), call, then destroy (`0x492556`).
- **`hd_text_fn` arguments:** use the SysV order. Pass the same six ints the game passes with the popup (24, 160, 88, 100, 1, 180), not zeros: Linux reads ints 2, 5, 6 and 7. Entry and kind are `short`, each in its own 8-byte stack slot, and the message is the 4th stack slot.
- **Unchanged:** the HD message objects (`0x238`, fields `+0x20C`/`+0x210`/`+0x220`/`+0x228`/`+0x22C`/`+0x230`), the message slots (`0x1628`; `+0x1D8`, `+0x1E0`, `+0x1EC`, `+0x1620`, `+0x1624`), the label's translated glyphs (`+0x230`, `+0x238`, `+0x240`, `+0x244`, `+0x248`, `+0x24C`, `+0x258`, `+0x260`), and `message_slots` at exactly the gGameState shift.
- **The counts:** these are separate named int16 globals (`gSmallTextCount`, `gLargeTextCount`, `gPowerUpDefCount`, `gDatabaseEntryCount`). They are not where the shift would put them, and they are not next to the Windows neighbors.

## Mapped by disassembly: item looks

The general-object and sprite fields the helper's item looks read and write (`native/src/looks.cpp`,
`game_layout.h`'s `obj` and `sprite`, and the stand-in naming's positions in `game_calls.cpp`),
which are fields, not `client/patterns.py` targets. For each, the Linux code that does what
`memory.md` describes for Windows ("What decides whether an item appears", "Other players' items",
"Live item objects"), and how that was shown. Addresses are the Linux binary's; "measured" means by
disassembly, as in the sections above. None was read in the running game but the painting of `sprite_texture`, seen as the AP logo on the file select ("Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19").

| Field | Windows | Linux | How found | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| general object's map cell: `+0x210` (row) and `+0x211` (column) | `+0x210`, `+0x211` | `+0x210`, `+0x211`, the same order | The general-object spawn `createEnemy(SpriteRec*, SpriteRec*, BossStruct*, short, char, char, char, double, double, char*)` `0x41A3A3` takes a slot at `gGameState+0x388DA0 + i × 0x5C8` (`0x41A608`–`0x41A62A`) and stores its 5th argument at `+0x210` (`0x41A8F2`–`0x41A8F6`) and its 6th at `+0x211` (`0x41A900`–`0x41A904`). The room loader `spawnScreen(char, char)` `0x5290B1` passes its own first and second arguments there, the cell's X and Y (`0x5293D0`–`0x5293ED`). The type-23 step reads them as the pickup reads an item object's `+0x3AC`/`+0x3AD`: the cell's item byte at `gGameState+0x15B69 + [+0x210] × 0x1E6 + [+0x211] × 9` (`0x43DD08`–`0x43DD45`; the `cell_state` row of the Lookup). Its drop calls `createUpgrade([+0x210], [+0x211], 1, …)` (`0x43E281`–`0x43E2AA`), which stores its first argument at the item object's `+0x3AC` and its second at `+0x3AD` (`0x53E668`–`0x53E67E`). | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) | **Matches**: `+0x210` plays the item object's `+0x3AC` (`obj::kGeneralRow`) and `+0x211` its `+0x3AD` (`obj::kGeneralColumn`). The 7th argument, the object's index among the cell's 8 placed objects, goes to `+0x212` (`0x41A90E`–`0x41A912`). |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing the item looks read or write.** The general-object fields (`+0x1D8`, `+0x210`,
  `+0x211`, `+0x32C`, `+0x32E`), the sprite fields (`+0x30`, `+0x40`, `+0x48`, `+0x120`,
  `+0x198`, `+0x1A0`, `+0x1A8`, `+0x1B0`, `+0x1BC`, `+0x1C4`, `+0x1D0`), the frame entry `0x38`,
  `item_frame_base`'s chain and the two first states are as on Windows.
- **The tables are named globals:** the types `gEnemyDefs` (`0x170`-byte records, the file's),
  the script steps `gEnemyActions` (`0x6C` bytes), their handlers `gEnemyActionProcs` (entry 23
  `PowerupEnemyAction`), and the cells' placed objects `gMapScreens` (`0x9A` bytes a cell, X
  major). The helper's looks name none of them.
- **Found on Linux and not in `memory.md`:** the object's place at the spawn (`+0x40`/`+0x48`
  from `+0x558`/`+0x560`, assumed on Windows for general objects); the type-23 step's state-3
  condition (its float `+0x44` = 1.0); and 18 script slots from `+0x32E`.
- **OpenGL, for the logo's painting:** the binary needs `libGL.so.1` (`readelf -d`: `NEEDED`)
  and imports `glBindTexture`, `glGetError`, `glGetIntegerv` and `glGetTexImage` from it
  (`nm -D`), so its OpenGL 1.1 functions are in the process's global scope, where the helper's
  `load_gl` finds them with `dlsym(RTLD_DEFAULT, …)`. The other four it uses
  (`glGetTexLevelParameteriv`, `glPixelStorei`, `glTexSubImage2D`, `glIsTexture`) are not the
  game's imports: they are assumed to resolve from the same library (the test PC's
  `libGL.so.1`, libglvnd's, exports all four: `nm -D`, 2026-11-18). The texture behind `sprite_texture` is painted as on Windows: the AP logo the helper paints into the font's glyphs showed on an AP save's file select entry (seen in the game, 2026-11-19, B1 on Linux, Flatpak Steam; the four functions resolved there).

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18), each row as its Status says; the painting of `sprite_texture` run in the game, 2026-11-19 (the AP logo on the file select: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19"), the rest not (other players' items' labels set, not looked at); assumed: that the objects' updates keep `+0x40`/`+0x48` current; the four OpenGL functions the game does not import resolved from its `libGL.so.1` on the test PC (the logo showed, 2026-11-19), and are assumed to elsewhere |
| Method | `memory.md`'s description of each field matched against `all.asm` (the Linux disassembly) and the symbol table (`nm -C`); two `.rodata` values (the float 1.0 at `0x68C650`, the string `Enemies` at `0x68D7D8`) read from the binary through its `LOAD` segments |
| Build | B1 (Linux), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-18, at the addresses each row cites. Each was read by hand, in one pass and with no second review: `createEnemy`, `spawnScreen`, `PowerupEnemyAction`, `createUpgrade`, `SWCloneSprite`, `SWDisposeSprite`, `SWSetSpriteVisible`, `SWMoveSprite`, `SWSetCurrentFrameIndex`, `SWUpdateParallaxSpriteWorld`, `IndexedColorDrawProc`, `loadStuff`, and the stores into `gEnemyActionProcs`. Re-checked by hand the same day: the cell's stores at `0x41A8F6` and `0x41A904` (`mov BYTE PTR [rax+0x210],dl`, `[rax+0x211]`), the type-23 step's `mov BYTE PTR [rax+0x32c],0x1` at `0x43DE0C`, and the draw loop's test of `+0x120` at `0x66CB51`–`0x66CB5A`. |

## Mapped by disassembly: traps

What the helper's traps write and patch (`native/src/trap.h`, `trap.cpp`; `Game::run_trap`),
against the Linux code that does what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("Ailments: the script
step that sets them", "The Phazo's disruption", "The Balorb's disorienting wave"). Addresses are
the Linux binary's; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above.

| What | Windows (B1) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the type-74 state handler, ops 4 and 5 | `0x46BE0`, ops at `0x47910` to `0x47AE6` | `ChangePlayerStateEnemyAction(SpriteRec*, EnemyAction*)` `0x41CF6C` | Op 4 (`cmp ax,0x4` at `0x41E333`): the duration from `GetRandom` (`0x41E369`), the shield branch on player `+0x103C` (`0x41E39B`–`0x41E3D8`, with `PlayGameSound` and the rumble), else `+0xDC2` set to the duration unless it is already longer and the state's float `+0x50` is 0 (`0x41E460`–`0x41E497`). Op 5 (`cmp ax,0x5` at `0x41E4AE`): AILMENTS (`gGameState+0x13724`) tested for 2 first (`0x41E4B8`–`0x41E4BF`); then, unless `+0xDC4` is already longer and the state's float `+0x50` is 0 (`0x41E52E`–`0x41E550`, as op 4's), `+0xDDB` (a byte) turns by 4 mod 8 with C's `%` while `+0xDC4` is 0 or less (`0x41E556`–`0x41E58E`), `+0xD20` (16-bit) becomes 2 if it is 1 or less (`0x41E594`–`0x41E5A9`), and `+0xDC4` takes the duration (`0x41E5B2`–`0x41E5BA`). | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) |
| the AILMENTS tests the trap's patches skip | `kHitGame` and `kWaveGame` in `trap.cpp`, B1's | `weaponCool` at `0x507FCB` (`0x35` bytes) and `0x50805B` (`0x38` bytes): each `movzx eax,[rip+d]` (AILMENTS), `cmp ax,1`, `jne` past the REDUCED effect (`+0xB`), the effect, a second load, `cmp ax,2`, `jne` past the store of 0 (`+0x33`, `+0x36`) | `trap.cpp`'s Linux `kHitGame` and `kWaveGame` read back from the binary through its `LOAD` segments (`client/elf.py`) equal those bytes with the loads' displacements masked, and each of the four loads leads to `0x9FAF64` (2026-11-18). The rows `ailments_hit_code` and `ailments_wave_code` above give the instructions. | measured (by disassembly and a read of the binary, 2026-11-18) |

### What differs from Windows

- **The patch shapes:** the unoptimized code loads AILMENTS into `eax` (`0F B7 05`, not `0F B7 0D`)
  and compares with `cmp ax, imm` (not `cmp cx, r12w`), and its effects go through `[rbp-0x18]`,
  so the patches are longer (`0x35`, `0x38`) and their jumps further in (`+0x33`, `+0x36`); the
  first jump of each is at `+0xB`, as on Windows. The patch is the same: each `jne` (`75`) made a
  `jmp` (`EB`).
- **Nothing else:** the player fields (`+0xDC2`, `+0xDC4`, `+0xDDB`, `+0xD20`, `+0x103C`) and the
  ops' order and tests are Windows'.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18); a received Disorient Trap and Disruption Trap went off and wore off in the game, 2026-11-19 (run 1: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19"); this world's own traps not run in it |
| Method | `memory.md`'s description matched against `all.asm`; the patch bytes compared with the binary's, read through its `LOAD` segments with `client/elf.py` |
| Build | B1 (Linux), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-18, at the addresses the table cites, read by hand in one pass; the docs review the same day re-checked the ops' addresses and the patch bytes. The bytes' comparison: `elf.parse(lab/linux-b1/Zexion).read(0x507FCB, 0x35)` and `.read(0x50805B, 0x38)` (`client/elf.py`), each load's 4 displacement bytes zeroed, equal to `trap.cpp`'s Linux `kHitGame` and `kWaveGame`; each load's target, its next instruction's address plus its displacement, `0x9FAF64` (a scratch script, not kept). |

## Mapped by disassembly: the map's cursor

What the helper's names on the map cursor read and call (`native/src/mapmarks.cpp`,
`MapCursorNames` and the helpers above it: `holds`, `map_menu`, `info_panel`, `cursor_cell`),
against what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("Map markers", "The cursor's name"). Addresses
are the Linux binary's; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. Here the top
menu (`gLastGameMenuP`) is a `GameMenuScreen`, its panels `GameMenu`s, and their children
`GameMenuItem`s (each a `SpriteRec`).

| What | Windows (B1) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the move procedure writes the cursor's name each call | `0x169E50`, with `0xA61C0(text, name, 0, 17)` | `MapMoveProc(SpriteRec*)` `0x619957`, with `alterSpriteString(text, string, 0, 17)` (`text_set_fn`) | Each call works out the cursor's square (below, `0x61A0A7`..`0x61A1B6`), then walks the 8 map markers (`gGameState+0x13760`, 14 bytes each: row, column, text index at +2; `0x61AE08`..`0x61B188`). A marker on the square writes its text into the info panel's child 1 and `" "` into child 3, then returns (`0x61AEC6`..`0x61AF76`). Otherwise the name's color is set to `0x15` (`0x61B1BF`); the player's square (`[gMainCharacterSpriteP]+0x107A` and `+0x107B`) gets CURRENT LOCATION (`0x61B1C4`..`0x61B230`); any other square goes by its `map_grid` byte (row × 54 + column, `0x61B293` on). Above `0xB` and at most `0x23`: value % 12 = 1 is the record's name, `[gPowerUpDefs] + 100 × ID + 0xA`, with the ID read from `gMapScreens` (`0x61B293`..`0x61B403`); 9 is, in a randomizer game (marker 1; otherwise nothing is written), UNKNOWN ITEM with Obscure Class on and flag 250 clear, else ADVANCED TECH (`0x61B903`..`0x61BAF6`); 7 is UNKNOWN ITEM (`0x61BB92`..`0x61BC81`); 2 is a record's name in a randomizer game (`0x61BDC9`..`0x61C21C`), else ADVANCED TECH (`0x61C41D`..`0x61C45B`); 5, 10 and 8 are NAVIGATION ROOM, SAVE STATION and AMMO SUPPLY (`0x61C4F8`..`0x61C8F3`); any other square gets `" "` (`0x61C994`). `0x1B` is the record's name in color 7 (`0x61B638`..`0x61B702`). All 14 writes into the name text (the marker's `" "` among them) pass `ecx` = `0x11`, `edx` = 0 and the info panel's child 3's `+0x1F0`. No branch between the entry and `0x61AE08` jumps past this code, so every call writes the name text, or leaves it alone only for a class-9 square outside a randomizer game. | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18), for the order and the writes; class 2's randomizer branch (a record's redirect `+0x54`, setting `+0x885`) was read but not followed |
| who calls the move procedure, and with what | `+0x168` from the object loop (`0x7ABF3`), each frame (live); `+0x178` from `0x46B4F` | `+0x168` from `SWProcessSpriteLayer(SpriteWorldRec*, SpriteLayerRec*)` `0x4CD709`: for each sprite of an unpaused layer, `mov rdi,rbx; call rax` (`0x4CD757`..`0x4CD761`). It runs on `gMenuSpriteLayerP` from `updateGame()` (`0x670054`) and `gameEventLoop()` (`0x671E38`, `0x671F5F`, each followed by `prepareScreenUpdate()`). `+0x178` is called by `SWTempSpriteMoveProc` (`0x4CCB67`, while it sits in `+0x168`) and `LaterSpriteMoveProc` (`0x4AC42E`, on the sprite at its `+0x1E8`) | Every caller loads `rdi` with the sprite alone and ignores `rax`. `MapMoveProc` keeps only `rdi` (`mov [rbp-0x98],rdi`, `0x619967`) and reads no other argument register. `SWProcessSpriteLayer` keeps the current sprite in `rbx` and the next in `r12` across the call (`0x4CD733`..`0x4CD766`), both callee-saved under SysV | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18), for the calls; once per frame by the loop's shape, not traced |
| the map's first panel and its first child | `[[current_menu]]` panel 0; its child 0 holds the handler in `+0x168` and `+0x178` | `DisplayMap(bool,bool)` `0x61DCBE`: `createGameMenuScreen` (`0x61EA56`), then `createGameMenu(0x10, 0, FrameOrNothing, screen, 0)` (`0x61EA7D`), which stores itself as the screen's panel 0 (`[[screen]+0]`, `0x56A79F`..`0x56A7B7`). Its child 0 (`[[panel+0x1E0]]`, `0x61EAB4`..`0x61EAF9`) gets entry 120 in both slots (`map_handler`) | `createGameMenuItem` (`0x56BD34`) stores its `GameMenu*` argument at the child's `+0x1D8` (`0x56BE5C`), and `MapMoveProc` takes its panel from there (`[child+0x1D8]` into `[rbp-0x58]`, `0x61998C`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) |
| the info panel `[[[panel+0x300]]+8]` | `[[[panel+0x300]]+8]`, read at `0x16B843`..`0x16B85F` and `0x169EF1`..`0x169F3B` | the same: the map screen's panel 1 | `createGameMenu` stores its screen at the panel's `+0x300` (`0x56A7BA`..`0x56A7C2`), so `[[panel+0x300]]+8` is the screen's panel 1, which `DisplayMap` creates with index 1 (`createGameMenu(5, 4, FrameOrArrow, screen, 1)`, `0x62086F`..`0x62088D`). `MapMoveProc` reaches it the helper's way: `mov rax,[rax+0x300]; mov rax,[rax]; add rax,0x8; mov rax,[rax]` (for example `0x61B3D0`..`0x61B3DE`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) |
| the scale: the double at `[[info+0x1F0]]+0x158`, 40.0 the fine scale | the same chain; 40.0 zoomed in (live) | the same chain and value: `MapMoveProc` reads `[[info+0x1F0]]+0x158` and compares it with `ucomisd` to the double at `0x695C20`, which is 40.0, before each use of the cursor's place (13 times, the cell's at `0x61A0A7`..`0x61A0E0`) | the constant read from the binary with `client/elf.py` | measured (by disassembly and a read of the binary, 2026-11-18) |
| the cursor: the panel's child 3, row `+0x1E6` and column `+0x1E4` (16-bit), and the arithmetic | the panel's fourth child; (`+0x1E6` + 5) / 10 and (`+0x1E4` + 4) / 8 zoomed in, else (+ 11) / 23 and (+ 8) / 17 | the same: `[[panel+0x1E0]+0x18]` | At 40.0, row = (`+0x1E6` + 5) / 10 (`imul 0x66666667; sar 2`) and column = (`+0x1E4` + 4) / 8 (`sar 3`, rounding toward 0) (`0x61A0E2`..`0x61A142`); otherwise (row + 11) / 23 (`imul 0xB21642C9; add; sar 4`) and (column + 8) / 17 (`imul 0x78787879; sar 3`) (`0x61A147`..`0x61A1B6`). Each load is `movzx` then `cwde`, so signed 16-bit. `moveGameMenuItem(item, a, b, true)` writes `+0x1E6` = a and `+0x1E4` = b (`0x56BF66`..`0x56BF7F`), and `MapMoveProc` puts the panel's child 2 at (row × 10, column × 8) at 40.0, else (row × 23, column × 17) (`0x61AC80`..`0x61ACE7`): the same axes; signed C division, as the helper's | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) |
| the name text: the info panel's child 3's label, 17 glyphs, count `+0x1D8`, glyphs `+0x228` | `[[[info+0x1E0]+0x18]+0x1F0]`, 17 glyphs | the same, 17 glyphs | `[[[info+0x1E0]+0x18]+0x1F0]` in all 14 writes above. `DisplayMap` creates that child as `createGameMenuItem(" ", 0x11, info, ...)` into `[info+0x1E0]+0x18` (`0x620EFA`..`0x620F3A`), and `createGameMenuItem` makes its label with `newSpriteString(text, n, n)` at `+0x1F0` (`0x56BE06`..`0x56BE1E`). `newSpriteString` stores n at `+0x1D8` (32-bit, `0x558978`) and the glyph array (`calloc`, 8 bytes a glyph) at `+0x228` (`0x558A18`). `alterSpriteString` stops at `+0x1D8` and sets each glyph's frame (`SWSetCurrentFrameIndex`, which writes `+0x1C4`, `0x4C8EAC`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing the helper reads:** every offset, the 17 glyphs, the scale's 40.0 and the cell
  arithmetic are the Windows ones (`mapmarks.cpp`'s constants, `kMapNameMax`, `label::kLength`,
  `label::kGlyphs`, `sprite::kFrameIndex`). The wrapper's shape (`native/linux/src/helper.cpp`,
  `map_hook`: the game's procedure with the child, then the helper's code) fits the one-argument
  SysV call.
- **The info panel's `+0x1F0` is not a widget's label.** On a `GameMenu` it is the second array
  `createGameMenu` allocates (`0x56A839`..`0x56A845`). Its entry 0 is a sprite `DisplayMap`
  clones from `gMasterSpritesP` entry 52 (`0x6208A2`..`0x6208C4`). The bytes the helper reads are
  the ones the game reads; only the comment's name for them (`widget::kLabel`) does not fit.
- **The writers that undo a wrap, by name:** the navigation room's reveal (Windows `0x16DA80`,
  `0x16D550`) is, on Linux, `DisplayMap` queuing `StartDecodingMenu()` `0x61DC3F` (through
  `gExitActionP`, delay 30: `0x621716`..`0x621724`). That puts `MapDecodeMoveProc` in the top
  menu's panel 0's child 0 with `SWSetSpriteMoveProc` (both slots, `0x61DC86`..`0x61DC9E`;
  `0x4CC5FB`) and queues `RestoreMapModeMenu()` (delay 150, `0x61DCA3`..`0x61DCB1`), which puts
  entry 120 back in both. One more writer: `readdSprite(SpriteRec*)` `0x4AECC0` sets `+0x178` and
  `+0x168` back from `gMoveProcs` by the index words `+0x180` and `+0x170`
  (`0x4AEDC7`..`0x4AEE38`); it is called only from `loadState()` (16 calls). Whether Windows has
  that one is not known. `MapCursorNames::still_wrapped` treats both slots rewritten as
  unwrapped, so either is handled as the reveal is on Windows.
- **Not in `memory.md`:** the class-9 branch reads Obscure Class (`gCurrentRando+0x8A4`, the
  section's settings `+0x87B` plus 41) and flag 250 (`gGameState+0x1E7`) with the marker 1
  (`0x61B9E8`..`0x61BA11`). This is the UNKNOWN ITEM seen on Windows under Obscure Class
  (`memory.md`: "the branch not traced"); that Windows tests the same is assumed.
- **The callers are named functions:** the sprite layer's loop (`SWProcessSpriteLayer`) where
  Windows has an unnamed object loop; the move procedure's address is a `gMoveProcs` entry, not
  a lone global (`map_handler`).

| Field | |
|---|---|

| Status | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18), each row as its Status says; not run in the game (neither run looked at the map's cursor or its names: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19") |
| Method | `memory.md`'s description matched against `all.asm` (`MapMoveProc`, `DisplayMap`, `createGameMenu`, `createGameMenuItem`, `newSpriteString`, `alterSpriteString`, `SWProcessSpriteLayer` and the other callers of `+0x168` and `+0x178`, found by searching `all.asm` for those offsets); the scale's constant read through the binary's `LOAD` segments with `client/elf.py` |
| Build | B1 (Linux), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-18, at the addresses the table cites, read by hand in one pass (`MapMoveProc` and `DisplayMap` cut into scratch files, not kept); `elf.parse(lab/linux-b1/Zexion).read(0x695C20, 8)` as a double, 40.0, and the strings the writes pass (`0x6951D4` CURRENT LOCATION, `0x6951E5` UNKNOWN ITEM, `0x6951F2` ADVANCED TECH, `0x693456` `" "`) read the same way Re-checked by hand the same day, and again by the docs review: `MapMoveProc`'s `alterSpriteString(…, 0, 0x11)` call (`0x61B3F6`–`0x61B403`), `SWProcessSpriteLayer`'s one-argument call of `+0x168` (`0x4CD757`–`0x4CD761`), the scale constant 40.0 at `0x695C20`, `[child+0x1D8]` at `0x61998C`, `DisplayMap`'s `mov edx,0x78` at `0x61EDAD`. |

## Mapped by disassembly: the helper's translated text

What the helper's translation code relies on beyond the targets of "Mapped by disassembly:
translations" (`native/src/translation.cpp` and `.h`: `rebuild_label` and its constants,
`hdlabel::`, `FontMetrics`, `hd_icon`, `PopupAreas`, `clear_mark`; `native/src/apitem.h`'s
icon sheets), against the Linux code that does what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("The
helper's calls in a translation", "Where a rebuilt label goes", "The translations' icons",
"Borrowed entries for popups", "Translated menus and the prompt"). Addresses are the Linux
binary's; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. Here a label is a
`SpriteString` (a widget's `+0x1F0`), and "the font" is `GameFont` at `gCurrentTranslation+0xD0`.

| What | Windows (B1) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the attach, and the place the game-mode panel, the prompt and the pause row pass it (`kAttachOffset`) | `0x8AC30(widget, _, entry, x, y, pixel_shown)` builds at the first pixel glyph's place plus x, y; the panel and the prompt pass −1.0 (`0x217D98`) | `generateSimpleTextForMenuItem(SpriteRec*, bool, int, float, float, int)` `0x550F16`; −1.0, the float at `0x695B28` | It reads SmallText's entry through `+0x58` and `+0x30` and does nothing for −1 (`0x550F4B`–`0x550F64`). It builds at `[[label+0x228]]` `+0x40` plus x and `+0x48` plus y (`0x550FBC`–`0x551006`), with the font and flag 0 (`0x55100E`–`0x551023`), then destroys the string (`0x55102F`). Last, `setSpriteStringVisiblity(label, pixel_shown)` (`0x551053`) sets `+0x1EC` to it (at most `+0x1D8`) and shows that many pixel glyphs, hiding the rest (`0x5689B7`–`0x568A52`). Callers passing `0x695B28` each way: the game-mode panel, which is part of `SaveFileMenu()` on Linux (354 at `0x62FA7B`–`0x62FA98`, 352 at `0x62FC7B`–`0x62FC8B`); the prompt, `SkipFinalEscapeOptionMenu()` (340 at `0x6169A4`–`0x6169C1`, 341 at `0x616A37`–`0x616A47`, 374 at `0x616B50`–`0x616B6D`, after `moveGameMenu` at `0x616B29`); the pause row, `refreshPauseFromOption` (139 at `0x618956`–`0x618973`). `generateSimpleLargeTextForMenuItem` `0x5510A9` does the same from LargeText (`+0x38`, `+0x60`), and passes its last argument as the builder's wrap flag (`0x5510CE`, `0x5511C8`). The float read from the binary. | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18). **Matches** |

| centering down (`kIconMiddle`; `Rebuild::keep_middle`) | `0x86660(label, y)`: top and bottom the first glyph's place + `+0xA0` and `+0xC0`, or + 4.0 (`0x217680`) each for an icon; widened over each shown glyph after it from the font or an icon sheet; moved by y − (bottom − top) · 0.5 − top, rounded for a pixel font | `verticalTranslationSpriteString(SpriteString*, float)` `0x549D04`; 4.0 the double at `0x695990` | First glyph: y + `+0xA0` and y + `+0xC0` if its `+0x30` is the font's, else y + 4.0 both (`0x549E02`–`0x549ED5`). For each glyph it copies the 64 bytes at the FreeType face's (`+0x98`) glyph slot's `+0x30` into its record (`0x54CDD3`–`0x54CE37`): FreeType's glyph metrics, eight `FT_Pos`, which is a C `long`, 8 bytes on Linux and 4 on Windows (inferred: the binary imports `FT_Load_Char`, `FT_Set_Pixel_Sizes` and `FT_New_Memory_Face`, and the 4-byte reading gives Windows' `0x20` and `+0x08`, `+0x10`). `generateText`'s advance for a font glyph: `[rec+0x20]` / (64.0 × scale) − `[rec+0x10]` / (64.0 × scale), each read as a 64-bit integer (`mov rax,[rax+0x20]`, `cvtsi2ss xmm0,rax`), times font `+0x40060` (`0x54EEBC`–`0x54EF73`; 64.0 the double at `0x695A08`). | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18); the FreeType reading inferred. **Differs:** the helper's `0x20`-byte records and 32-bit reads at `+0x08`/`+0x10` measure wrong widths on Linux. ### What differs from Windows

- **The font's glyph records are twice the size.** `[font+8]` holds `0x40`-byte records, and the
  builder takes a glyph's advance from its `+0x20` less its `+0x10`, both 64-bit. Windows'
  are `0x20`-byte records with 32-bit values at `+0x08` and `+0x10`, so `FontMetrics` reads the
  Linux layout on Linux (`kGlyphEntry`, `kGlyphFrom`, `kGlyphTo`, `GlyphField`;
  `native/linux/tests/translation_tests.cpp` reads a glyph's advance from such a record, and
  fails with the Windows layout): with Windows', the popups' wrapping and centering
  (`layout_popup`, `centered_lines`) would be wrong for every font glyph; the space and the
  icons, whose advances come from elsewhere, would not. The records are FreeType's glyph metrics, `long` fields
  (inferred, above).
- **The builder drops what does not fit.** Its loop stops once the glyph count reaches `+0x240`,
  so characters past the label's room are not built at all. `memory.md` has Windows laying them
  over the last glyph. The helper never builds past the room.
- **The centering's widening stops at a hidden glyph,** across and down. Down, it also compares
  a glyph's top with its quad's first x (`+0x98`). The first does not matter for a text the
  helper has just built, every glyph of which is shown. The second can make the game's top of a
  text differ from `vertical_extent`'s. `keep_middle` measures the old text and the new one the
  same way, its own, so it keeps the middle by that measure; whether that puts the Feats title
  where the game's own centering would is not worked out.
- **Named on Linux:** the attach is `generateSimpleTextForMenuItem`, the centering across is
  `centerTranslationSpriteString`, down is `verticalTranslationSpriteString`, and the label
  layout is `moveSpriteString`. The game-mode panel is built inside `SaveFileMenu()`, and the
  prompt with Skip Escape and Final Battle? is `SkipFinalEscapeOptionMenu()`.
- **Not in `memory.md`:** `refreshPauseFromOption` centers the pause row's attached text across
  (with 0.0) after attaching it, which the helper's rebuild of the count does too (`pause.cpp`,
  `.center`). Whether Windows' `0x168910` does the same is not recorded.
- **Unchanged:** the constants `rebuild_label` uses (−1.0, 160, 2.0, 4.0, the corners `+0x98`,
  `+0xA0`, `+0xA8`, `+0xC0`), the `hdlabel::` offsets and size, the wrap widths, the space and
  scale, the factor `+0x40060`, the icon characters' frames and sheets, and the mark.

## Mapped by disassembly: the disks' count

What the helper's OS Install Disks count relies on beyond the targets of "Mapped by disassembly:
menus and UI" (`native/src/equip.cpp`, `EquipmentHook` and `EquipmentScreen`: the disk added to
the equipment screen's Energy Tank panel and named in its description box; `native/src/pause.cpp`,
`PauseDiskRow`: the KEYS row turned into the count; `native/src/scan.cpp`, `ScanText`: the
scanner's line), against the Linux code that does what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("The
equipment screen", "The pause screen's KEYS row", "The scanner"). Addresses are the Linux
binary's; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. A panel is a `GameMenu`
(the symbol table types it `SpriteRec*` in some signatures), a widget a `GameMenuItem`, a
screen a `GameMenuScreen`.

| What | Windows (B1) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

The helper's own `std::malloc` and `std::free` (`equip.cpp` off Windows) are those same functions, one glibc in the process (assumed: the dynamic linker's one symbol scope, not traced; the GOT slots are not used, which matters since they are lazily bound) |

### What differs from Windows

- **The branch's bytes:** `75 3C` (`jne +0x3C`), not `75 2E`; the patch is the same one byte,
  `75` to `EB`. `pause.cpp` keeps the Linux pair off Windows.
- **The panel's arrays come from `calloc`,** not `malloc`, both glibc's, and the game frees them
  with glibc's `free` (`removeGameMenu`). The helper's arrays and frees are glibc's too, with no
  need for the GOT slots (`equip.cpp` calls `std::malloc` and `std::free` off Windows).
- **Callbacks take no arguments** (`StandardMenuAction`'s bare call, "Mapped by disassembly:
  menus and UI"): the EQUIPMENT wrapper must not use its arguments. Handlers and the way back
  (`+0x68`) do get theirs, in `rdi` (and `esi`).
- **Not in `memory.md`:** `EquipmentMenu` sets the pause screen's `+0x68` to
  `refreshPausePalette` (`BasicReturn`, then `setSpritePalette(7, 0x11)`) before it builds
  (`0x5E331A`–`0x5E3328`), and its own screen's to `EquipmentMenuReturn` (`0x5E72A6`–`0x5E72AD`).
  The helper's wrapper is only on the pause screen's `+0x68` while OPTION MENU is open over it,
  when EQUIPMENT cannot be chosen, so the store does not meet it. Whether Windows' `0x12BA90`
  does the same is not recorded. The description box's child 1 is created with index 0, as
  child 0; the helper reads them by place.
- **Unchanged:** every offset, count and place the helper uses (the widget's `+0x1D8` to `+0x2C8`,
  `+0x1E4` and `+0x1E6`, the panel's `+0x1E0` to `+0x1F8`, `+0x2C6`, `+0x2E4`, `+0x2F8`; the 7
  panels; 3, 0x11 and 0x88 glyphs; events 0 and 3; `+0x230`; definition 34 and the record's
  frame; the 2 panels, 9 sprites and 4 glyphs of the KEYS row; frames 384 to 387; 144, 208 and
  the icons' places; the sprite's `+0x128`, `+0x148`, corners and `0x38`-byte frames; `+0x68`
  with the menu; 32-byte Text entries).

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18), each row as its Status says; run in the game, 2026-11-19 (run 2: the pause screen's disk row, the count notices, the file select's DISK count, this world's own disks counted at their pickup with a client attached: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19"); not run: the scanner's line, the equipment screen's disk, own disks with no client; assumed: entry 12's text and frame 829's entry (the shared data files, not read on Linux), and that the helper's `std::malloc`/`std::free` are the game's glibc ones |
| Method | `memory.md`'s description of each Windows step matched against `all.asm` (`createGameMenuItem`, `createGameMenu`, `createGameMenuScreen`, `moveGameMenuItem`, `EquipmentMenu`, `EquipmentFrame`, `removeGameMenu`, `removeGameMenuScreen`, `removeGameMenuItem`, `SWCloneSprite`, `SWDisposeSprite`, `SWCloseSprite`, `SWMoveSprite`, `SWSetCurrentFrameIndex`, `SWSetSpriteIndexedDrawProc`, `SWNormalHardwareDrawProc`, `PauseGame`, `refreshPauseFromOption`, `refreshPausePalette`, `exitMenuCount`, `loadStuff`'s Text read, `DisplayMessageAreaEvent`); the strings and the floats and doubles read from the binary with `client/elf.py`; the frees of `+0x1A8` by an `awk` scan of `all.asm` |
| Build | B1 (Linux), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it, and `native/src/equip.cpp`, `pause.cpp`, `scan.cpp` as of 2026-11-18 |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm` and `lab/linux-b1/Zexion`, 2026-11-18, at the addresses the table cites, read by hand in one pass with no second review (`EquipmentMenu`, `EquipmentFrame` and `PauseGame` cut into scratch files, not kept). Read from the binary: `" 99"` at `0x694538`, `"KEYS"` at `0x69519D`, `"Text"` at `0x68DB52`; the doubles `0x695A28` 16.0 and `0x695DA8` 12.0; the floats `0x695CF8` 120.0, `0x695BE4` 136.0, `0x695E60` 184.0, `0x695CEC` 200.0, `0x695CD8` 176.0, `0x6959B8` 160.0, `0x695B70` 188.0 |

## Mapped by disassembly: the RETURN HOME prompt

What the helper's RETURN HOME WITH THE DISKS? relies on beyond the routines' and events' targets of
"Mapped by disassembly: new games, events, the map, feats" (`native/src/mission.h` and
`mission.cpp`: `fade_state::`, `kFadeRecord` and its fields, `kFadeSide`, `kNormalLevel`,
`kKeepLevel`, `kBackStep`, `kPromptChildren`, `kQuestionCenterBy`, the routines 2, 4 and 15),
against the Linux code that does what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("The mission prompt",
"Telling play from menus, the title screen and death"). Addresses are the Linux binary's;
"measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. On Linux the fade is a
`TileAnimator` (the symbol table's name for the type its functions take).

| What | Windows (B1) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the fade's block (`fade`; `fade_state::kTarget` `0x93`, `kStep` `0x9F`, `kCountdown` `0xAB`, `kSize` `0xAC`) | a struct at `+0x3E2AE8`: three arrays of 12 signed bytes, target `+0x93`, step `+0x9F`, countdown `+0xAB`, index 0 the global level; its saved copy at `+0x3E2C38` | `gGameState+0x13448` (`0x9FAC88`, the shift), a `TileAnimator` of `0x150` bytes; the same three arrays; its saved copy at `gGameState+0x13598` (`0x9FADD8`, the shift of `+0x3E2C38`), right after it | `setAutoFade` (below) stores layer n's step at `gGameState+0x134E7` + n (`+0x9F`), countdown at `+0x134F3` + n (`+0xAB`) and target at `+0x134DB` + n (`+0x93`) (`0x4A4786`–`0x4A481C`). `processTileAnimator(TileAnimator*)` `0x4A563E`, called on it, reads the same three as `[p+0x9F]`, `[p+0xAB]`, `[p+0x93]` (`0x4A5C47`–`0x4A5D1D`). `clearTileAnimator(TileAnimator*, bool)` `0x4A4EED` sets all 12 to target 4, step 0, countdown 0 (`0x4A4F8F`–`0x4A4FD3`), and `copyTileAnimator(TileAnimator*, TileAnimator*)` `0x4A51D6` copies them (`0x4A52B9`–`0x4A532D`) with the struct's other fields up to the double at `+0x148` (`0x4A55C2`). With `gTimePause` (`0x12F7749`) set, `updateGame` skips the transition, the clock and the stepper alike (`0x670035`–`0x67007C`); the builder clears it (`0x61674E`) and `exitMenuCount` puts back the value it saved (`0x5596F4`–`0x5596FB`). | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18). **Matches** for a menu opened in play. **Differs** (not in `memory.md` for Windows): the clock also runs under a paused layer while `+0xAC0` is set, which a menu opened before any input after a load could see; the helper counts its black only with no menu open, so this does not reach it. Assumed: that `gTimePause` is Windows' `+0x2305EF` (the same role: cleared by the builder, a path without the stepper) |

### What differs from Windows

- **The callbacks take no arguments.** The game calls YES and NO as `void (*)()`
  (`StandardMenuAction`, `0x58B943`); the Windows helper's YES calls the game's NO with
  (widget, event). The Linux helper's `mission_yes_hook` already takes none and passes none.
- **The builder sets the level and the fade's aim in one call,** `setAutoFade(0, 4, −1, 6)`, where
  Windows has a call and three stores; the result is the same: level −1, target 4, step and
  countdown 6.
- **The builder, the pause and the ending's wrapper leave the transition elsewhere:** the builder
  sets `gExitActionP` to 0, `PauseGame` to `StandardExitMenu`, `EndingCutsceneThing` to 0. Not in
  `memory.md`. The helper reads only "is it the opener, the wrapper, or the idle routine", so none of
  these changes what it does.
- **The builder and the pause save the fade only while the main layer is not paused already.**
  Not recorded for Windows. Both run from play here, where it is not.
- **The ending sets level 4 through `clearFades(1)`,** which also sets the saved levels to 4 and
  repaints through `setFade`, not by a lone byte write; the level the helper reads afterwards is
  the same, 4.
- **`frame_clock` has one more condition:** it runs under a paused layer while `gGameState+0xAC0`
  (the play time's hold after a load) is set. Not recorded for Windows; outside the helper's count.
- **Named on Linux:** the stepper is `processTileAnimator`, the type 1 handler
  `FadePaletteAreaEvent` with `setAutoFade`, the level setter `setFade`, the save and restore
  `copyTileAnimator`, `saveIndexColors` and `loadIndexColors`, the menu close `exitMenuCount`, NO
  `StandardExitMenu`, YES `fastSkipEscapeExit`, the ending's own transition
  `EndingCutsceneThing`, the message clear `ClearGameScene`, the warp `SetCameraMode`.
- **Unchanged:** `fade` at the shift, `fade_state::` (`0x93`, `0x9F`, `0xAB`, `0xAC` within the
  `0x150`-byte block), event 3667's record, event 3666's delay 60 and routine 15, the type 1
  handler's use of `+0x2` to `+0x8` and `+0xC`, `0xFE` keeping the level, a step of 0 setting the
  target at once, NO's close leaving level −1 toward 4 a step every 6, the three children and the
  question's 30 glyphs, the centering by −4.0, the ending's level 4 and its clearing of the
  messages, the pause's save and restore, the transition and its block of the pause, and
  `frame_clock` standing still while a menu has paused the layers.

## Mapped by disassembly: received scrap's drops

What the helper's received Scrap Metal, Scrap Energy and Scrap Chunk drops (and the Energy
Shield's spawn, the helper's `shield`, which runs the same code; what the shield relies on
beyond it: "Mapped by disassembly: received Energy Shields") rely on beyond the events' targets of "Mapped by
disassembly: new games, events, the map, feats" (`native/src/game_calls.cpp`: `kSpawnRecord`,
`kChunkFadeRecord`, `kItemEvents`, `Game::pump_drops`, `run_item_event`, `item_event_ready`,
`held_for_ending`, `settled`), against the Linux code and data that do what `memory.md`
describes for Windows ("Received Energy Shields", "Received scrap"). Addresses are the Linux
binary's; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above, or by a read of the
installed data where the row says so.

From each load to the next call, a scratch sweep finds no store through a register and no pointer kept on the stack or in a global. `gAreaEventCount` is written only by the loader's `SDL_RWread` (its one `lea`, `0x4B6130`). `gAreaEventProcs` has 165 references: the 45 stores in `SetupAreaEvents()`, and 120 `lea`s each followed by a load of an entry (111 into `r8`, 9 into `rax`). | the rest of what `pump_drops`, `held_for_ending`, `settled` and `item_event_ready` read | as `memory.md` records each | as the Lookup records each | `save_loaded`, `menu_state`, player `+0xCEA` (`in_play`); `transition_timer` (`gExitDelay`, 32-bit; `transition_queued`); `flags` 250 and 211 (`ending_flag`; `fastSkipEscapeExit` sets 250 and 185 to 212: "Mapped by disassembly: the RETURN HOME prompt"); the RETURN HOME state (`mission_`: that section); `rando_marker` and `rando_blob` (`origin_loaded`: the section's item table `+0x26C` and start `+0x310`, and `+0x8A0`, which `generateRando` reads to make the location count 154, or 155 when it is set, `0x5ABDC5`–`0x5ABDDA`, as `rando::locations` counts); `item_records` and `+0x40` (above); `event_table`, `event_count` and slots 1 and 11 of `event_handlers` (above); the map side 54 (`cell_state`'s `0x1E6` = 54 × 9; the start table's 54 × 54 walk, `start_count`). The handlers' addresses pass `in_image_code`: both lie in `.text` (`0x40C670`–`0x68B995`). | measured (by disassembly, each as its Lookup row says, 2026-11-18); open: `menu_state` in play with a mouse (its row), where a 1 would make the drops wait, not misfire; not traced on Linux: what clears flag 211 as the ending begins (on Windows the ship's events and the ending: `memory.md`, "The ending: flag 250") |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing the helper's drops rely on.** The five records are byte for byte the helper's
  constants, the handlers are in slots 11 and 1, the spawn's place, object and pool, the sound,
  the next event, the pickup's dispatch and its argument order all match, and the table is
  written only by its loader. The Linux helper already maps `event_table`, `event_count`,
  `event_handlers` and `item_records` by symbol (`native/linux/src/helper.cpp`) and calls
  `pump_drops` from its frame hook; no change to `run_item_event` or `item_event_ready` follows
  from this section.
- **The spawn is `createEnemy`, passed the cell and a slot of −1.** It stores the cell at the
  object's `+0x210`/`+0x211` and the −1 at `+0x212`, and with −1 touches no cell state. What
  Windows' `0x44B60` is passed besides the object and the place is not in `memory.md`.
- **The runaway check counts in `[gSpriteWorldP]+0x68`,** zeroed before each sprite's move
  procedure. Not recorded for Windows. The helper's call comes outside any move procedure, so it
  adds to what the last one left; it would be refused (silently: no drop, and the helper reports
  it spawned) only were that count already 3,000, or 2,999 for the fade after 3550: assumed not
  to happen, not measured.
- **The loader reads with `SDL_RWread`, not `fread`** (as the events section has), and
  unoptimized code reloads the pointer at each use: 282 loads where Windows has 94, of the same
  kinds.
- **Named on Linux:** the handler `CreateObjectAreaEvent`, the check `AreaEventRecursionCheck`,
  the spawner `createEnemy`, the loader `loadStuff`, the setup `SetupAreaEvents`, the pickup
  `playerCollideProc`.

The installed data files are B1's: their fingerprints (the first 16 hex digits of their SHA-256) equal the Windows install's (`builds.md`, "B1 on Linux"), and the records read match the helper's constants, which came from the Windows copy |
| Method | `memory.md`'s description of each Windows step matched against `all.asm` (`CreateObjectAreaEvent`, `AreaEventRecursionCheck`, `createEnemy`, `FadePaletteAreaEvent`, `SetupAreaEvents`, `loadStuff`, `playerCollideProc`, `SWProcessSpriteLayer`, `EditorUpdateKeys`); scratch Python over `all.asm`, not kept: every reference to `gAreaEvents`, `gAreaEventProcs` and `gAreaEventCount` by function and kind, from each load of `gAreaEvents` the instructions to the next call (stores through a register, pointers kept), and in each function taking an `AreaEvent*` the record's stack slot followed through each reload (stores through it, copies kept, calls it is passed to; the sweep's hits checked by hand: each a store into the flags or `gGameUnlocks` indexed by a record field, not into the record); the installed `Files/AreaEvents` and `Files/Enemies` read through `tools/protected.py`'s `game_dir()` (read only) by scratch scripts that compared records 3548 to 3553 with `kSpawnRecord` and `kChunkFadeRecord` as `item_event_ready` builds them and read the four types' palette and first state; the strings `0x68C518` and `0x68CDD0` read from the binary |

The installed `Files/AreaEvents` (152,802 bytes, SHA-256 beginning `bb8946fd16dd`; records 3548 to 3553, and every record naming 3548, 3549, 3550, 3552 or 3553 next) and `Files/Enemies` (2,686,584 bytes, SHA-256 beginning `8e41d2ef5665`; types 2010, 2016, 2017 and 2018), read only |

## Mapped by disassembly: received Energy Shields

What a received Energy Shield relies on beyond its spawn, against the Linux code that does what
`memory.md` describes for Windows ("Received Energy Shields"). The spawn is the helper's
`Game::spawn_shield` (`native/src/game_calls.cpp`), which runs event 3553 through
`run_item_event` as the drops do: the record, the type 11 handler and `createEnemy` are the
previous section's. What follows is the shield itself, as the client's check reads it
(`client/game.py`: `SHIELD_LINK`, `SHIELD_POOL_TYPE`, `OBJECT_HANDLE`, `HANDLE`, `shield_up`):
the player's link at `+0xFE8`, the pool the link points into, the object's handle at `+0x1D8`,
and the game's own test. Addresses are the Linux binary's; the player state is
`*gMainCharacterSpriteP`, `gGameState+0x1CD50` (`0xA04590`: "`gGameState` has the Windows
layout"), so its link is at `0xA05578` and the link's handle at `0xA05580`. "Measured" means by
disassembly, as in the sections above, or by a read of the installed data where the row says so.

| What | Windows (B1) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

The handler's player is `gMainCharacterSpriteP` (`0x41CFBB`). | measured (by disassembly and a read of the installed `Enemies`, 2026-11-18); assumed (that 2018's states reach 13791 by `+0x40`). Its 52 calls are all in `loadState`, which `updateGame()` (`0x66FFC8`) and `gameEventLoop()` (`0x671EE3`) call behind key tests (`gKeys`), with `saveState()` beside it (`0x66FF61`, `0x671E0D`): a state kept in memory, not the save file. Which keys, and whether play reaches it, is not traced. | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18). **Matches** for the types and pools; that Windows' `0x2019BB` is in the same routine is assumed from its role |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing the client's check reads.** The link at player `+0xFE8` (pointer), its handle at
  `+0xFF0` (serial, 32-bit), `+0xFF4` (type, 16-bit), `+0xFF6`/`+0xFF7` unwritten and `+0xFF8`
  (slot, 32-bit), pool type 2, the object's handle at `+0x1D8`, `+0x1DC` and `+0x1E0`, and the
  game's own test are Windows'. The Linux player state is `gGameState+0x1CD50`, so the link the
  client reads through `inventory` (`gGameState+0x1DDDE` less `0xA6`) is `gGameState+0x1DD38`,
  the address `loadState`'s fix-up names. No change to `client/game.py` or to `spawn_shield`
  follows from this section.
- **The link is copied by a call:** `setIndexedSpritePtr`, where Windows' code copies the three
  fields inline; the game's test is `verifyIndexedSpritePtr`, the fix-up `restoreIndexedSprite`,
  and the shield is made by `newMissileSprite`.
- **The fix-up is in `loadState`,** a state kept in memory and restored behind key tests, not
  the save file; the save file's routines make a new shield instead (`LoadGame`, as `memory.md`
  has for Windows' load routine, and `SaveGame` and `SaveNoOpenMapGame`, which `memory.md` does
  not record for Windows).
- **Recorded here, not in `memory.md`:** the step that makes the shield (action 13791, type 74,
  op 32), its 80 and its cap of 320, the serial counter's address and that it is shared, the
  pool's cursor, and `processBomb`'s look by strength.
- **Named on Linux:** `ChangePlayerStateEnemyAction`, `newMissileSprite`, `setIndexedSpritePtr`,
  `verifyIndexedSpritePtr`, `restoreIndexedSprite`, `AttachIndexedSprite`, `ClearPlayer`,
  `ClearGameScene`, `loadState`, `LoadGame`, `SaveGame`, `MissileRemoveProc`, `processBomb`,
  `playerFlash`.

| Field | |
|---|---|

What the helper's Intel Chips (`native/src/intel.cpp`: `kAreaMap`, `kOwnName`, `kOwnEvent`;
`IntelChip::watch`, `restore`) rely on beyond `item_records` (the Lookup), against the Linux code
and the installed data that do what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("Intel Chips").

|---|---|---|---|---|
| the records, as loaded | the `Objects` file's power-up records, read as they are | `loadStuff` reads the file's power-up records with one `SDL_RWread` of count × 100 bytes into a `calloc`'d table (`0x4B62DC`–`0x4B6311`), its pointer stored once in `gPowerUpDefs` (`0x4B62E9`): the file's bytes, as they are | disassembly | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) |
| record 49, the Area Map | kind 34, frame 25, 6 ticks, 2 frames, icon 0, palette 3, inventory 46, redirect 0, pickup class 1, named AREA MAP, event 3551 (`kAreaMap`) | the same, read from the installed `Files/Objects` at byte 1,028 + 49 × 100 (`loadStuff` reads two 16-bit counts first, `gGeneralObjectCount` then `gPowerUpDefCount`, at `0x4B6243`–`0x4B626F`; the file's are 64 and 51; then the 64 general entries of 16 bytes and the 51 records: 4 + 1,024 + 5,100 = 6,128 bytes): every field as `kAreaMap`, AREA MAP, event 3551 | a read of the installed file (scratch Python, not kept) | measured (2026-11-18) |
| the pickup with the event off | a record's event −1 runs nothing | the pickup skips a record whose `+0x40` is −1 ("Mapped by disassembly: received scrap's drops", the pickup's call) | that section | measured (by disassembly) |

  byte for byte (`builds.md`, "B1 on Linux": SHA-256 beginning `72ea406ad4ff`, 6,128 bytes), the
  file `kAreaMap` was measured from.
- **Not swept:** whether any code writes the records after the load (the area events' table had
  that sweep: "Mapped by disassembly: received scrap's drops"). The helper's `matches(kAreaMap)`
  check, before each write, leaves a record that reads otherwise alone.

## Mapped by disassembly: the ASSIST lock

What the helper's ASSIST lock (`native/src/menu.cpp` and `.h`, `AssistLock`, with the layouts in
`native/src/game_layout.h`; `native/README.md`, "The assist menu") relies on beyond the targets
of "Mapped by disassembly: menus and UI" (`widget_handler_fn`, `assist_menu_fn`,
`current_menu`) and what "the helper's translated text" maps, against the Linux code and the
installed art that do what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("The assist menu", "Where a
rebuilt label goes", "Translated menus and the prompt"). Addresses are the Linux binary's;
"measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. "Draw proc n" is `gSpriteDrawProcs`
entry n (`palette_table`), what `memory.md` and `menu.cpp` call palette n:
`SWSetSpriteIndexedDrawProc(sprite, gSpriteDrawProcs, n)` (`0x4CD3B0`) stores the entry at the
sprite's `+0x198` and n, 16-bit, at its `+0x1A0` (`0x4CD3D2`–`0x4CD3FC`).

| What | Windows (B1, `memory.md`) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing the lock reads or writes.** Every offset `menu.cpp` and `game_layout.h` use agrees:
  widget `+0x1E0`, `+0x1F0`, `+0x2C0`, `+0x2C8`; panel `+0x1E0`, `+0x1E8`, `+0x1EC`, `+0x2DC`,
  `+0x2F8`, `+0x228`; label `+0x1D8`, `+0x228`; sprite `+0x40`, the tint at `+0x68` to `+0x80`
  (red, green, blue), `+0x84`, `+0x198`, `+0x1A0` (16-bit), `+0x1A8`, `+0x1B0`, `+0x1BC`,
  `+0x1C4` ("item looks"), `+0x1C8`, `+0x1D0`; menu `+0`, `+8`, `+0x78`. So do its constants: 12
  glyphs, the padlock eighth, styles 5 and `0x15`, draw proc 0, SmallText 137. No change to
  either file is needed for Linux.
- **No game call of its own.** The lock writes fields; its callback slot gets back `AssistMenu`'s
  address, which the Linux helper takes from the symbol table (a `void(*)()`, called with no
  arguments: "Mapped by disassembly: menus and UI"). In a translation it goes through
  `rebuild_label`, whose Linux differences "the helper's translated text" and "translations"
  record.
- **A fourth way to build the options menu again:** `resetOptionWorkshops`, from the
  translation-mod screens. The lock finds its entry afresh each frame, so it covers it.
- **Not in `memory.md`:** ASSIST built at style `0x17` under a deaths test; STYLE: AUTO's gray as
  the current global palette's entry 5; VERSION 1.20's count of 0; `refreshOptionsTitlePosition`
  moving the last child; the `+0x248` skip byte; the controls screen's tint copy for translated
  text. None changes what the lock does.
- **Named on Linux:** the options menu is `OptionsMenu`, the rebuild `resetOptions`, the
  controls screen with STYLE: AUTO `OtherControllerOptionsMenu`, the translated glyphs' draw
  `SWHDTextSpriteHardwareDrawProc`. `menu.cpp`'s comments cite the Windows addresses
  (`0x124D30`, `0x1628B0`, `0x8AAB5`).

| Field | |
|---|---|
