## Mapped by disassembly: the title screen's lines

What the helper's title lines (`native/src/title.cpp` and `.h`, `Game::watch_title` in
`native/src/game_calls.cpp`; `native/README.md`, "The title screen's lines") rely on beyond the
targets of "Mapped by disassembly: menus and UI" (`text_new_fn`, `text_place_fn`,
`text_link_fn`, `draw_layer`), `menu_state` and `save_loaded` (the Lookup) and what "the
helper's translated text" maps (`rebuild_label`, `hdlabel::`), against the Linux code that does
what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("The title screen", "Where a rebuilt label goes",
"Telling play from menus, the title screen and death"). Addresses are the Linux binary's;
"measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. "The title's list" is the sprite
layer `*gStatsSpriteLayerP` (`draw_layer`); "the HD list" is where an HD font's translated
glyphs go.

| What | Windows (B1, `memory.md`) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |

| the list and its links (`kListHead`, `kListTail`, `kOwner`, `kNext`, `kPrevious`) | the holder at `draw_layer`: head `+0x18`, tail `+0x20`; each object's list `+0`, next `+0x10`, previous `+0x18`; the title routine links its line at the tail only if `[text]` is 0, inlined (`0x93885`) | the same, through `SWAddSprite` | `SWAddSprite(SpriteLayerRec*, SpriteRec*)` `0x4CB258`, only when the sprite's `+0` is 0 (`0x4CB278`–`0x4CB28E`): the old tail's `+0x10` = the sprite, or the holder's `+0x18` with no tail (`0x4CB290`–`0x4CB2AD`); the sprite's `+0x18` = the old tail, `+0x10` = 0; the holder's `+0x20` = the sprite; the sprite's `+0` = the holder (`0x4CB2B1`–`0x4CB2DD`). `SWInsertSpriteAfterSprite(sprite, after)` `0x4CBB52`, only when the sprite's `+0` is 0: after's next's `+0x18` = the sprite, the sprite's `+0x10` = after's next, after's `+0x10` = the sprite, the sprite's `+0x18` = after, the holder's `+0x20` = the sprite when it is now last, the sprite's `+0` = after's `+0` (`0x4CBB66`–`0x4CBBEF`). `SWRemoveSprite` `0x4CB2E6` undoes it through the holder's `+0x18` and `+0x20`, then sets `+0` to 0 and the 16-bit `+0x8` to `0xFFFF`, leaving the sprite's own `+0x10` and `+0x18` (`0x4CB2F6`–`0x4CB389`). `SWCloneSprite` clears `+0`, `+0x10` and `+0x18` of every sprite it makes (`0x4C8B32`–`0x4C8B49`), so a new text is in no list. | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19). **Matches**: `make_line`'s link at the tail is, store for store, `SWAddSprite`'s |

A 1 on the title would make the helper hold its lines unmade, or its translated glyphs hidden, until a key is pressed (the same code on Windows: not checked there) |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing title.cpp reads or writes.** The list's holder (`+0x18`, `+0x20`) and links (`+0`,
  `+0x10`, `+0x18`), the text's fields (`+0x178`, `+0x180`, `+0x1D8`, `+0x1DC`, `+0x1E4`,
  `+0x1E8`, `+0x1EC`, `+0x228`), the glyphs' (`+0x120`, `+0x198`, `+0x1A0`, `+0x1C4`, `+0x1D0`)
  and the `hdlabel::` fields agree, and so do the game line's values: 18 glyphs, 32 columns, the
  © frame `0xA9` for the `C`, `GALLANT LEAF` last, draw proc 26, style `0x15`, tag `0x79`, its
  place (88, 120) on the 320 × 224 screen. The three calls are SysV, which title.cpp's typed
  pointers handle. No change to `title.cpp`, `title.h` or their layout constants is needed for
  Linux.
- **The tail link is a function on Linux:** `SWAddSprite` (`0x4CB258`), where Windows inlines it
  (`0x93885`). `make_line`'s own link does the same stores.
- **The tag call sets the live move procedure too:** `+0x168` and `+0x170`, with `+0x178` and
  `+0x180`. title.cpp copies only the latter, so its lines have none on Linux. The procedure,
  `GameStringMoveProc`, does nothing with the values the game's line and the helper's hold (a
  color index of −1, no typewriter, no countdown), so nothing changes.
- **The HD list is `*gMenuSpriteLayerP`**, and the menus have layers of their own, drawn between
  the title's list and the HD list (the order measured, the drawing from the head assumed).
  `memory.md` reads Windows' menus as adding their objects to the title's list; on Linux they do
  not. title.cpp's rule (lines made, and translated glyphs shown, only with `menu_state` 0)
  holds either way.
- **`menu_state` and the mouse:** at the title the mouse can set it to 1 (the open item of its
  Lookup row), which would keep the lines unmade or their translated glyphs hidden until a key
  is pressed.
- **Not in `memory.md`:** the countdown at `+0x1E8` and the color index at `+0x1E4` as
  `GameStringMoveProc` reads them; `HideTranslationSprites` called by `createGameMenuScreen` and
  `exitMenuCount` as well as the builders; the font glyphs as `0x1D8`-byte allocations, past
  which `look` reads (checked reads, as on Windows).
- **Named on Linux:** the title routine is `TitleScreenSecondPart`, the scene's clear
  `ClearGameScene`, the list's clear `SWClearAllSpritesInLayer`, a text's disposal
  `SpriteStringClearProc`, the HD list's hiding `HideTranslationSprites`. title.cpp's comments
  cite the Windows addresses (`0x92300`, `0x93885`).

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly and a read of the binary, 2026-11-19), each row as its Status says; the title screen's lines run in the game, 2026-11-19 (run 1: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19"); assumed: the layers drawn from the head of their list, and what runs routine entry 3 (the title animation's end, as on Windows); open: `menu_state` with the mouse moved on the title |
| Method | `memory.md`'s "The title screen", "Where a rebuilt label goes" and "Telling play from menus, the title screen and death" matched against `all.asm`: `TitleScreenSecondPart`, `TitleScreen`, `TitleExitMenu`, `TitleExitMenuEscSkip`, `SetUpExitActions`, `newSpriteString`, `SWCloneSprite`, `SWAddSprite`, `SWInsertSpriteAfterSprite`, `SWRemoveSprite`, `addSpriteString`, `setSpriteStringVisiblity`, `SWSetCurrentFrameIndex`, `SWSetSpriteIndexedMoveProc`, `SWSetSpriteDisposalProc`, `SWSetSpriteIndexedDrawProc`, `setSpriteStringDrawProc`, `SWSetSpriteVisible`, `GameStringMoveProc`, `SpriteStringClearProc`, `ClearGameScene`, `SWClearAllSpritesInLayer`, `generateText`'s glyph links, `HideTranslationSprites`, `createGameMenuScreen`, `createGameMenu`, `addGameMenu`, `addGameMenuItem`, `SetUpParallax`, `SWAddParallaxLayer`, `SWInsertParallaxLayerBefore`, `SWPauseSpriteLayer`, `SaveFileMenu`, `SaveGameMenuAction`, `SaveGamePlayerCursor`, `OptionsMenu`, `exitMenuCount`, `UpdateKeys`, `handleMouseModeActivationCheck`, `handleMouseGameModeDeactivationCheck`, `checkMouseMoveActive`, and `setUpInterface`'s stores into `gMoveProcs`, read by hand; the string read from the binary's `LOAD` segment by a scratch Python script, not kept; censuses by scratch shell scripts over `all.asm`, not kept: the constant stores to `gCursorMode`, the callers of `HideTranslationSprites`, `ClearGameScene` and `SWClearAllSpritesInLayer`, the references to `gMenuSpriteLayerP`, and the readers and writers of `+0x168`, `+0x178` and `+0x180` |
| Build | B1 (Linux: `lab/linux-b1/Zexion`, SHA-256 `cd64cd9ec469…`), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-19, at the addresses the table cites, read in one pass with no second review. From the binary: `C2025 GALLANT LEAF` and its 0 at `0x6933DE` |

## Mapped by disassembly: the Feats guard's Steam and load test

What the helper's Feats guard (`native/src/feats.h` and `.cpp`; `native/README.md`, "Feats and
achievements in AP saves") relies on for its Steam stand-in and its load test, beyond the Lookup
rows `stats_context` and `context_init_slot` ("Mapped by disassembly: new games, events, the map,
feats"), `current_slot`, `rando_marker`, `save_loaded`, `slot_array` and `rando_slots`, against
what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("Feats and achievements": the Steam stub and "at load";
"Save data lives in Zexion.exe's static data"; "The file select"). It is mapped for the route the
Linux guard is to take: the helper, preloaded, exports `SteamInternal_ContextInit` and forwards
every call to the real one except, while blocking, a call for `stats_context` (route 2 in
`context_init_slot`'s notes). Addresses are the Linux binary's unless they name
`libsteam_api.so`, the copy in the game's folder (SHA-256 `b343f8be18ad…`, 386,864 bytes), read
from a scratch copy; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above.

| What | Windows (B1, `memory.md`) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the calls of `SteamInternal_ContextInit` in the game | 69 calls through the import slot, inlined at each use: 68 with `stats_context`, one with `SteamUtils010`'s context | two, one in each Steamworks accessor: `SteamUserStats()` `0x63C084` and `SteamUtils()` `0x4C5E48` | Each is `lea rdi,[context]` (`0x63C08C`: `0x6BBB70`; `0x4C5E50`: `SteamUtils()::s_CallbackCounterAndContext` `0x6BBA50`), `call SteamInternal_ContextInit@plt` (`0x63C093`, `0x4C5E57`), `mov rax,[rax]`, `ret`. Those two `lea`s are the only references to either context in `all.asm`, and no loaded section holds their addresses (the binary's only other copies of `0x6BBB70` and `0x6BBA50` are `.symtab` entries, at file offsets `0x2CDA00` and `0x2CA640`). `SteamUtils()`'s context starts `(0x4C5E1D, 0, 0)`, its init loading `SteamUtils010` (`0x68D670`) | measured (by disassembly and a read of the binary, 2026-11-19) |
| who calls the accessors | every use calls the import, then `mov rcx,[rax]; mov rax,[rcx]; call [rax+slot]`; no result tested | `SteamUserStats()` only from `setTheAchievement(int)` `0x5459E3` (`0x5459F2`, `0x545A22`) and `clearTheAchievement(int)` `0x545A38` (`0x545A47`, `0x545A77`); `SteamUtils()` only from `ClearLocalPreferences()` (`0x4C2D5C`) | `setTheAchievement` is called from 23 places (`playerCollideProc` 6, `FeatsMenuItemMoveProc` 6, `EditorUpdateKeys` 2, `UpdateKeys` 2, `BonusMenu`, `DatabaseMenu`, `EndingCutscene`, `mapExplore`, `setDatabaseUnlock`, `tallyDatabaseEntriesForAchievement`, `tallyMapForAchievement`), `clearTheAchievement` from 6, all in `RemoveFeatRelatedAchievements(int)`. `ClearLocalPreferences` is called from `LoadPreferences()` (`0x4C570F`, `0x4C57DD`; called from `main` at `0x66D735`, before the game loop) and `EditorUpdateKeys()` (`0x4E235F`); it calls `SteamUtils()`'s slot `0x110` and tests the `bool` it returns (`0x4C2D61`–`0x4C2D75`) to set `gLocalPreferences+0x21D` | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19) |
| the slots called, and their results | `0x30` SetAchievement (with a name), `0x38` ClearAchievement (with a name), `0x48` StoreStats (after each); the slot names assumed from Steamworks' layout | the same three; the names measured from `libsteam_api.so`; no result read | `setTheAchievement`: `this` the interface pointer in `rdi`, the name `achievementStrings[n]` (`0x6BD4E0`) in `rsi`, `call [vtable+0x30]` (`0x5459F7`–`0x545A20`), then the accessor again and `call [vtable+0x48]` with `rdi` only (`0x545A22`–`0x545A34`); `clearTheAchievement` the same with `0x38` (`0x545A4C`–`0x545A89`). Each ends `leave; ret` after its last call, reading neither `rax` nor `al`. `libsteam_api.so`'s flat API thunks (`mov rax,[rdi]; jmp [rax+N]`) name the slots: `SteamAPI_ISteamUserStats_SetAchievement` `0x309B0` (`0x30`), `_ClearAchievement` `0x309C0` (`0x38`), `_StoreStats` `0x309E0` (`0x48`); its `ISteamUserStats` thunks reach 44 slots, `0` (`_GetStatInt32`) to `0x158` (`_GetAchievementProgressLimitsFloat`). `ISteamUtils`' `0x110` is `SteamAPI_ISteamUtils_IsSteamRunningOnSteamDeck` (`0x2FE90`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19: the slots, the results unread, the flat API's offsets); assumed (the three return a `bool`: Steamworks' headers, not in either binary) |
| what `SteamInternal_ContextInit` returns | the context's third quadword, the cached `ISteamUserStats` pointer, is what each use reads; the interface fetched again only when the callback counter changes (Steamworks' accessor design, assumed) | a pointer to the context's third quadword (`context+0x10`), which the accessor reads | `libsteam_api.so` `0x2DBF0`: compares the context's `+0x8` with a library global (`0x2DBF7`–`0x2DC09`); when equal, returns `rdi+0x10` (`0x2DC02`, `0x2DC0F`); else, under `pthread_mutex_lock`/`unlock` (`0x2DC2A`, `0x2DC53`: PLT slots `0x4A468` and `0x4A480`), checks again, calls the context's first quadword with `rdi+0x10` (`0x2DC3F`: the init, which stores the interface pointer there) and stores the global in `+0x8` (`0x2DC48`), then returns `rdi+0x10`. So the game's call reads `[[return]]` as the object and `[[[return]]]` as its vtable, as the Windows stub's holder, object and vtable are laid out | measured (by disassembly of `libsteam_api.so`, 2026-11-19); the counter's meaning (Steamworks' design) not read |
| what else calls it (route 2's reach) | not recorded | the game's PLT only; `libsteam_api.so` calls its own definition directly | `libsteam_api.so` exports `SteamInternal_ContextInit` (`nm -D`: `T`, `0x2DBF0`) and has no relocation naming it (`readelf -r`); its 44 calls of it are `call 0x2DBF0` from its own flat API (`SteamAPI_SteamUserStats_v013` at `0x3093B`, the other `SteamAPI_Steam*_v*` accessors and a few more), which the game does not import, so an export from the helper does not reach them. Of the game folder's other libraries (`libfmod.so.13`, `libfreetype.so.6.20.2`, `libGLEW.so.2.1`, `libSDL2-2.0.so.0`, `libSDL2_image-2.0.so.0`, `libz.so.1.3.1`), none contains the name (`grep -c`). `libsteam_api.so` is linked `BIND_NOW` (`FLAGS`), the game is not ("`context_init_slot`") | measured (the game's folder, 2026-11-19); open (Steam's own libraries, such as the overlay preloaded after the helper and the client library `libsteam_api.so` loads: not read; a call of theirs with another context passes through all the same) |
| the threads that reach the accessors | not recorded | the game's thread: both are reached from `main` (`SteamUserStats` ← `setTheAchievement` ← `UpdateKeys` ← `gameEventLoop` ← `main`; `SteamUtils` ← `ClearLocalPreferences` ← `LoadPreferences` ← `main`), the thread that also runs `SDL_GL_SwapWindow`, the helper's frame call | The binary imports no thread or timer API (`nm -D`: no `pthread_create`, `SDL_CreateThread`, `SDL_AddTimer` or `std::thread`; only libstdc++'s weak `__pthread_key_create`), no SDL event watch, filter or hint callback, no audio callback (only `SDL_AudioQuit`), no `dlopen`, and no Steam callback API (no `SteamAPI_RunCallbacks`, no `SteamAPI_RegisterCallback`: Steam's callbacks are never run). The only game code it hands a library to run is FMOD's two PCM callbacks, `pcmreadcallback` `0x4CDFEA` and `pcmsetposcallback` `0x4CDFCE`, stored into `fsetUpMusicstuff`'s `FMOD_CREATESOUNDEXINFO` (`0x4CE179`–`0x4CE18E`); by direct calls neither reaches either accessor | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19: the imports, the callbacks' direct calls); assumed (indirect calls not followed: one in what the PCM callback reaches, `Music_Emu::emu_play`'s; and that FMOD runs nothing else of the game's) |
| Steam's start | not recorded | `main` (`0x66D69D`–`0x66D6EF`): `SteamAPI_RestartAppIfNecessary(0x33C3FE)` (3392510, the app id), returning 1 when it asks for a restart; then `SteamAPI_Init()`, and on failure a message (`puts`, `SDL_ShowSimpleMessageBox`) and a return of 1; then `init()`, `SetUpBasePath()`, `LoadSaveGames()` | `SteamAPI_Init()` `0x672355` is the SDK's inline one, calling `SteamAPI_InitEx(nullptr)` `0x67236E`, which calls `SteamInternal_SteamAPI_Init(0x696C08, nullptr)` (`0x672397`; the interface list there starts `SteamUtils010`), success when it returns 0. Nothing imports `SteamAPI_Shutdown`. ### What differs from Windows

- **One accessor per interface, not 69 inlined uses.** The game reaches
  `SteamInternal_ContextInit` through its PLT from `SteamUserStats()` and `SteamUtils()` only,
  with the context in `rdi` (SysV), and reads the interface pointer from what it returns. Both
  accessors are reached from `main`'s thread alone: the binary starts no thread, registers no
  callback but FMOD's two PCM ones (which reach neither), and never runs Steam's callbacks.
- **Route 2 reaches the game's call alone.** `libsteam_api.so` calls its own
  `SteamInternal_ContextInit` directly, and no other library in the game's folder names it, so a
  definition exported from the preloaded helper is called by the game's PLT slot only (Steam's
  own libraries not read). The helper's definition can forward to `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT, …)`, which is
  `libsteam_api.so`'s (`0x2DBF0`), and must never call through the game's GOT slot, which then
  holds the helper's own definition. Its first call comes at start-up, before the helper is
  configured, from `LoadPreferences` when it clears the preferences (`SteamUtils()`): it must
  pass through then. The decision for `stats_context` (`rdi` equal to `0x6BBB70`) is made on the
  thread that also runs the helper's frame call.
- **The stand-in's vtable:** the game calls slots `0x30`, `0x38` and `0x48` and reads no result,
  so a vtable of 10 entries returning 0 would do; the Windows helper's 64 cover the 44 that
  `libsteam_api.so`'s flat API reaches.
- **The load is `LoadGame`, called from four routines** (five sites), where `memory.md` names one
  load routine; its last act is a call of `tallyMapForAchievement`, not a tail jump. A load can
  also reach 16CompleteReport, through a room's enemy, after the marker and `save_loaded` are set
  (`memory.md` names only 15Explorer at load). It copies the section only when the record's
  marker is 1.
- **Not in `memory.md`:** the save file's two 16-bit versions (1000) before the record and before
  the section, and a file without the first read from its start; `LoadGame` storing
  `gCurrentSaveGame` into `gPreferences+0x228`; `SteamAPI_RestartAppIfNecessary` and
  `SteamAPI_Init` at the start of `main`, with no `SteamAPI_Shutdown` or
  `SteamAPI_RunCallbacks`; `SteamUtils010`'s one use, `IsSteamRunningOnSteamDeck`, deciding
  `gLocalPreferences+0x21D`.

|---|---|

| Method | `memory.md`'s "Feats and achievements", "Save data lives in Zexion.exe's static data" and "The file select" matched against `all.asm`: `SteamUserStats`, `SteamUtils`, their init functions, `setTheAchievement`, `clearTheAchievement`, `ClearLocalPreferences`, `main`, `SteamAPI_Init`, `SteamAPI_InitEx`, `LoadGame`, `LoadGameExit`, `LoadCheckpointExit`, `LoadTeleportSave`, `StartNewGameExit`'s calls and section copy, `SaveGameMenuAction`'s press, `tallyMapForAchievement`, `SaveGameData`'s marker and `save_loaded` stores, `ClearSaveGame`, the delete, `LoadSaveGames`' file loop, every reference to `gSavedRandos` and every store to `gCurrentSaveGame` and the marker, `fsetUpMusicstuff`'s callbacks, read by hand; the binary's imports (`nm -D`), dynamic section (`readelf -d`) and section headers (`readelf -S`); strings, `achievementStrings[14]` and the contexts' initial quadwords read from the binary's `LOAD` segments, and the contexts' addresses searched for in the file, by scratch Python scripts, not kept; `LoadGame`'s, the PCM callbacks' and `main`'s direct-call graphs by a scratch Python script over `all.asm`, not kept; `libsteam_api.so` read out of the game's folder into the scratch directory and disassembled (`objdump -d -C -M intel`), its imports, exports and relocations read (`nm -D`, `readelf -d`, `readelf -r`), not kept; the other libraries in the game's folder searched for the name (`grep -c`) |

## Mapped by disassembly: the Feats of Natu screen's note

What the helper's note on the Feats of Natu screen (`native/src/feats.cpp`, `feats_screen_note`
and the hint's stand-in; `native/README.md`, "Feats and achievements in AP saves", "The Feats of
Natu screen" and "The hint at the title") relies on beyond the targets of "Mapped by
disassembly: menus and UI" (`current_menu`, `widget_handler_fn`), what "the ASSIST lock" maps
(panels, widgets, the close), "the title screen's lines" (labels, glyph frames) and "the
helper's translated text" (`rebuild_label`, LargeText), against the Linux code and the installed
data that do what `memory.md` describes for Windows ("Feats and achievements": MISSION COMPLETE,
the screen, its title and hints; "The assist menu"). Addresses are the Linux binary's;
"measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. "This run's byte" of Feat k is
`gGameUnlocks+0x43E` + k, "on record" `gGameUnlocks+0x42A` + k (Feats 0 to 19); "the secret
row" is the fifth panel.

| What | Windows (B1, `memory.md`) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| from an ending to MISSION COMPLETE | `0x948A0` goes on to the Feats of Natu screen (`[+0x3CF640]`) if flag 251 is set, else to the title (`[+0x3CF630]`) or, with the Data Disc, Ending B (`[+0x3CF658]`); yet AP endings (flag 251 clear) reach the screen: how that fits is not traced | the same test picks **a cutscene**, not the screen: flag 251 entry 10 `ZipToExtraEndingCutscene`, the Data Disc entry 13 `ZipToDataDiskCutscene`, else entry 8 `ZipToCredits`; MISSION COMPLETE comes after the credits whatever the flag | `EndingCutscene` sets `EndingCutsceneThing()` (`0x55A959`) as its exit and any-key action (`0x55B2B2`–`0x55B2C0`), which tests flag 251 (`gGameState+0x1E8`, `0x55A9B6`) and sets the any-key action to `gExitActions+0x50` (`0x55A9C1`), else with the Data Disc (player `+0x10BA`) `+0x68` (`0x55A9E0`), else `+0x40` (`0x55A9F0`); `SetUpExitActions` stores `ZipToCredits` at `+0x40`, `ZipToExtraEndingCutscene` at `+0x50` and `ZipToDataDiskCutscene` at `+0x68` (`0x63BC97`–`0x63BCE4`). The credits' scroll procedure `CreditsCameraScrollProc(bool)` queues `FadeOutPostGameStats()` (`0x5312AD`) as the exit and any-key action (`0x5316FC`–`0x531711`) or calls it (`0x531753`); that queues routine entry 6, `PostGameStats()` (`gExitActions+0x30`), with delay 40 (`0x5312CE`–`0x5312DC`), clears the any-key action and stores `0xFF` in `save_loaded` (`0x531316`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19); not traced: each cutscene's way to the credits (`ExtraEndingCutscene`, `DataDiskCutscene`, `displayCredits`). Linux's entry 10 is the extra ending's cutscene, not the screen, so `memory.md`'s reading of `[+0x3CF640]` as the screen is probably wrong for Windows too (the entries match by offset: "new games, events, the map, feats", `routine_table`); Windows' entry 10 is not checked. The screen also needs the save to stay this seed's until the credits store `0xFF` in `save_loaded` (assumed: what `ZipToCredits`, `DataDiskCutscene` and `displayCredits` write on the way is not traced; were the section or the marker cleared first, the note would not show, though no Feat or achievement comes of it). ### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing `feats_screen_note` reads or writes.** The panel counts (4, or 5 with a secret row of
  5), panel 0's 3 children and EXIT's 1, the rows' 6 to 7 and 5 to 7, MISSION COMPLETE's 10 and
  8 lines under the screen at `+0x78`, the title's live widget with `StandardMenuAction`, its
  114-glyph label in 38 columns, the texts of 220 and 219 and where each sits, the hint 8 lower,
  the glyphs' frame, place and font, and the panel's y all agree. No change to `feats.cpp` or
  its constants is needed for Linux; its translated title goes through `rebuild_label`, whose
  Linux differences "the helper's translated text" records.
- **The flag-251 test after the ending picks a cutscene.** On Linux routine entry 10 is
  `ZipToExtraEndingCutscene`, so `memory.md`'s reading of Windows' `[+0x3CF640]` as the Feats of Natu screen does not hold here (and probably not on Windows either: not checked there): the screen comes after the credits and MISSION COMPLETE
  whichever cutscene ran, which is why AP endings reach it.
- **The screen runs with no save loaded:** `FadeOutPostGameStats` stores `0xFF` in
  `save_loaded` before MISSION COMPLETE.
- **Not in `memory.md`:** what adds ASSISTS USED (an assist setting above 0 as a menu closes,
  the speed key, or a save state's load); the conditions for hiding Feats 0, 1, 4 and 13
  and for the hints 218, 336 and 337; the secret row's code byte as `gPasswordActive`, set with
  13SecretCode and cleared only at start-up.
- **Named on Linux:** MISSION COMPLETE is `PostGameStats`, the way on `FadeOutFeats` and
  `FeatsToTitle`, the screen `FeatsMenu`, the Feats' reveal `FeatsMenuItemMoveProc`.
  `feats.cpp`'s comments cite `memory.md`'s Windows facts.

| Method | `memory.md`'s "Feats and achievements" and "The assist menu" matched against `all.asm`: `EndingCutsceneThing`, `SetUpExitActions`, `CreditsCameraScrollProc`, `FadeOutPostGameStats`, `PostGameStats`, `BasicExitCommand`, `updateGame`'s exit action and speed key, `exitMenuCount`, `loadState`, `SaveGameData`, `LoadGame`, `FadeOutFeats`, `FeatsToTitle`, `FeatsMenu`, `moveGameMenuItem`, `moveGameMenu`, `SWMoveSprite`, `moveSpriteString`, `newSpriteString`, `FeatsMenuItemMoveProc`, `setUpInterface`'s store into `gMoveProcs`, `TitleScreen`'s fold of the Feats, `loadSprites`, `loadSpriteFromSpriteDef` and `SWCreateSpriteFromSingleFileXY`, read by hand (`FeatsMenu` cut into a scratch file, not kept); censuses by scratch shell scripts over `all.asm`, not kept: the stores to this run's Feat bytes, to `gPasswordActive` and to a panel's `+0x1E8`, the references to `gGameState+0x13726` to `+0x13730`, the installs of move procedure `0x8F`, and the branches into `FeatsMenuItemMoveProc`'s calls; strings and `achievementStrings` read from the binary's `LOAD` segments, and `Files/Text` (entries 218, 219, 220, 336 and 337, after the 6-byte header and 448 labels of 32 bytes) and `Files/Sprites` (definition 1) read from the install, found through `tools/protected.py`'s `game_dir()`, by scratch Python scripts, not kept |

## Mapped by disassembly: the file select's AP logo and disk counts

What the helper's rewrite of the save entries (`native/src/files.cpp`, `FileEntries`, and
`Game::watch_files`; `native/README.md`, "AP saves on the file select" and "The OS Install Disks
goal") relies on beyond the targets of "Mapped by disassembly: menus and UI" (`file_panel_fn`,
`file_entry_fn`, `copy_entry_fn`, `delete_entry_fn`), what "the disks' count" maps (panels,
widgets, `SWMoveSprite`'s box), "the title screen's lines" and "the Feats of Natu screen's
note" (labels, glyph frames, the font), "item looks" (palette 26, `IndexedColorDrawProc`; the
logo's painting) and "the Feats guard's Steam and load test" (`gSaveGames`, `gCheckpointSaves`),
against the Linux code and the installed data that do what `memory.md` describes for Windows
("The file select"). Addresses are the Linux binary's; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the
sections above. Glyph i of an entry is row i / 29, column i % 29; "the logo's cells" are glyphs
16, 17, 45 and 46, "the keys block" glyphs 85, 86, 114, 115, 143 and 144.

| What | Windows (B1, `memory.md`) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the three screens' panel 0 and its entries (`kEntries`, `children_of`, `file_panel_fn`) | the file select's panel 0 holds a page's three entries as children 0 to 2; COPY FILE and DELETE FILE have the same entries | the same: in all three, panel 0 is `createGameMenu(4, 2, SaveGamePlayerCursor, menu, 0)`, children 0 to 2 the entries and child 3 the screen's title, 3 selectable | `SaveFileMenu()` `0x62E648`: panel 0 (`0x62E745`), child 3 `createGameMenuItem(" SELECT FILE", 12, …, StandardMenuAction, …)` (`0x62E94D`; the string at `0x6954A1`), then for i 0 to 2 the entry (below), `+0x1EC` less 1 (`0x62ED4A`–`0x62ED51`), `moveGameMenu(panel, 64, 24)` (`0x62ED68`). `ClearFileMenu()` `0x6223DC` and `CopyFileMenu()` `0x6241BE` do the same (panel 0 `0x62244D`, `0x62422F`; child 3 `" DELETE MODE"` `0x69528B`, 12 glyphs, `0x62263E`, and `"  COPY MODE"` `0x6952EC`, 11, `0x624420`; `+0x1EC` `0x622A12`, `0x6247F4`; the panel at (64, 24), `0x622A29`, `0x62480B`), taking their entries' slots from the menu below them, `[gLastGameMenuP]+0x78`'s panel 0 (`0x622703`–`0x6228FC`, `0x6244F0`–`0x6246DE`): the file select's, which stays open under them | measured (by disassembly and reads of the binary, 2026-11-19). **Matches**: `watch` finds three entries (`children_of` reads the capacity, 4) under `file_panel_fn` in each of the three menus, the file select's still wrapped under COPY FILE and DELETE FILE |

How a level below 0 darkens a palette 26 glyph is not traced (`setFade`, as above); that the logo darkens with the rest is assumed from Windows |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing `files.cpp` reads or writes.** The three menus' panel 0 under `file_panel_fn` with
  the three entries first, the 145 glyphs in 29 columns, `+0x1F8`, `+0x230` and `+0x2C8`, event
  `0xF` as an `int` after the game's fill, the keys block's glyphs 85, 86, 114, 115, 143 and 144
  and their frames `0x81` to `0x84` or 0, their palette 2 and style `0x15`, the logo's cells 16,
  17, 45 and 46 at frame `0x20` in palette 26 and style `0x15`, the rows 8 apart with y and the
  box as doubles, the empty font cells and the 40-frame new game all agree. No change to
  `files.cpp` or `files.h` is needed for Linux.
- **The block an entry shows** is the slot's checkpoint record (`gCheckpointSaves`) while that is
  in use, else the save's (`gSaveGames`); `memory.md` says the slot's first block, which on
  Windows may be the same choice (not recorded). `in_use` reads the same two fields either way.
  `Game::ap_file_slots` reads `gSaveGames` and `gSavedRandos` by slot, not the entry's block.
- **The fills that bypass the handler** are `BackoutFromDifficulty`, `BackoutFromClear` and
  `BackoutFromCopy` (a choice undone, a page turned during one, a copy finished, DELETE ALL);
  copies and deletes otherwise refill through the handlers (`syncSaveGameMenu`,
  `SaveFileReturn`). The watch rewrites those at the next frame call; whether a frame shows the
  game's glyphs first is not known on either OS.
- **Not in `memory.md`:** row 4's columns 0 to 10 at style `0x14`; the keys test over an empty
  slot's block; the label's line height set by `newSpriteString`; the empty slot reading in use
  through the new game's 40 frames; the fades' arguments, the new game's reaching its level at
  frame 30, 10 frames before the helper's logo is all white (cosmetic: `new_game_white` could end
  at 30, not required).
- **Named on Linux:** the fill is `refreshGameFile`, the screens `SaveFileMenu`, `CopyFileMenu`
  and `ClearFileMenu`, the number writer `displayNumberInSpriteString`. `files.cpp`'s comments
  cite `memory.md`'s Windows facts.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly and reads of the binary and the installed `Sprites.png`, 2026-11-19), each row as its Status says; the AP logo seen on the file select in the game (2026-11-19, B1 on Linux, Flatpak Steam), so the helper's painting of `sprite_texture` ("item looks") and its glyphs showing work there; the file select's DISK count following the grants, seen in the game, 2026-11-19 (run 2: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19"); assumed: style `0x15` white with the file select open (the logo showed; its color was not looked at, so the assumption in the "glyphs' looks" row stands); assumed: the fades' effect on palette 26 glyphs (`setFade` not traced); not traced: what calls the back action, and whether the delete clears a slot's key bytes |
| Method | `memory.md`'s "The file select" matched against `all.asm`: `refreshGameFile`, `displayNumberInSpriteString`, `setSpriteStringVisiblity`, `newSpriteString`, `moveSpriteString`, `SWMoveSprite`, `SaveFileMenu`, `CopyFileMenu`, `ClearFileMenu`, `SaveGameMenuAction`, `CopyGameMenuAction`, `ClearGameMenuAction`, the four select actions, `BackoutFromDifficulty`, `BackoutFromClear`, `BackoutFromCopy`, `syncSaveGameMenu`, `SaveFileReturn`, `SaveGamePlayerCursor` (no write to an entry's glyphs), `updateGame`'s exit action and `setFade`'s calls, read by hand (cut into scratch files, not kept); a census of `refreshGameFile`'s callers and of the `0xF` calls by `grep` and `awk` over `all.asm`; the strings read from the binary's `LOAD` segments, and `Files/Sprites.png`'s alpha read with Pillow from the install, found through `tools/protected.py`'s `game_dir()`, by scratch Python scripts, not kept |
| Build | B1 (Linux: `lab/linux-b1/Zexion`, SHA-256 `cd64cd9ec469…`), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it, and `native/src/files.cpp`, `files.h` and `game_calls.cpp` as of 2026-11-19 |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-19, at the addresses the table cites, read in one pass with no second review. From the binary: the entry strings at `0x693850` (145 characters) and `0x69383E` (15), the label's text at `0x695298`, the titles at `0x6954A1`, `0x69528B` and `0x6952EC`. The installed `Files/Sprites.png` (1,051,762 bytes, SHA-256 beginning `91d1e9422e40`, the one "item looks" read): the six cells 0 opaque pixels |

## Mapped by disassembly: the major-item list

What the helper's fill of the major-item list (`Game::watch_pickups`, `src/game_calls.cpp`;
`pickups` and `rando::started_with` in `src/game_layout.h`; `native/README.md`, "Grants", "The
major-item list") relies on beyond the targets it reads (`pickup_list`, `item_records`,
`rando_blob`, `inventory`: the Lookup), against the Linux code and the installed data that do what
`memory.md` describes for Windows ("The pickup record holds the major-item list") and
`save-format.md` for the save ("Major items are a list in pickup order at 0x9333"). Addresses are
the Linux binary's; "measured" means by disassembly, as in the sections above. "The list" is
`gGameState+0xAE0` (26 codes), "the length" `gGameState+0xAFA` (list `+0x1A`); in a
`SaveGameRec` they are `+0x9331` and `+0x934B` (file `0x9333` and `0x934D`: "The save record is
the file less its first two bytes").

| What | Windows (B1, `memory.md`) | Linux (B1) | How found | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the pickup's append (`pickups::listed`, `kMax`, `kCount`, `record::kIcon`, `record::kSize`) | the pickup's item branch (`0x1F761` to `0x1F7AC`) appends the touched item's record's icon (`+0x08`; the record is the object's `+0x1E8`, the item ID) to the list and raises the length, when the icon is not 0, `0x6E` or `0xE8`, the item is 3 to 26 or 32 to 44, and the length is below 26 (`cmp al, 0x1A; jge`) | the same | In the pickup, `playerCollideProc(CollideRec*, CollideRec*, dPoint*, char)` `0x64086D`, after the inventory's update and `refreshPlayerColors` (`0x64279D`): the record `[gPowerUpDefs] + id × 100` with the id the item object's 16-bit `+0x1E8` (`[rbp-0x210]`, the object whose `+0x3AC` and `+0x3AD` give the cell the same branch then marks, `cell_state`); its icon `+0x08` 0 skips (`0x6427D7`–`0x6427DD`), the id at most 2 skips (`0x6427F1`), the icon `0x6E` (`0x642834`) or `0xE8` (`0x642875`) skips, the id above 26 and at most 31 skips (`0x64288B`–`0x6428A3`), above 44 skips (`0x6428B3`); the length, a byte compared signed, above 25 skips (`cmp al,0x19; jg`, `0x6428B9`–`0x6428C2`); then the icon is stored at the list + the length (sign-extended, `0x6428FA`–`0x642911`) and the length raised by 1 (`0x642914`–`0x64291E`). Every jump out goes to `0x642924`, the cell's mark | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19). **Matches**: `listed` (icon not 0, `0x6E` or `0xE8`; item 3 to 26 or 32 to 44), the append while the length is 0 to 25 (`kMax` 26), the length at list `+0x1A` (`kCount`) as a signed byte, the icon at the record's `+0x08` (`kIcon`) in records of 100 bytes (`kSize`) |
| the load | `0x9DB8E` to `0x9DCE0`: the length, then the 26 codes, from the save block | the same | `LoadGame(SaveGameRec*)` `0x560EE5`: the length from the record's `+0x934B` (`0x561532`–`0x561539`), then for i 0 to 25 the code from `+0x9331 + i` (`0x56153F`–`0x561570`), with no test of either | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19; the list's copy first, 2026-11-18: "The save record is the file less its first two bytes"). **Matches** |
| the save | `0x1FDA99` to `0x1FDBEB`: the 26 codes, then the length, into the slot's block | the same, into the record each caller passes | `SaveGameData(SaveGameRec*, bool)` `0x4AB890`: for i 0 to 25 the code to the record's `+0x9331 + i` (`0x4AC17D`–`0x4AC1AE`), then the length to `+0x934B` (`0x4AC1B0`–`0x4AC1BB`); the function has no early return before them (its jumps are its own loops). Its nine callers: `SaveGame()` (`0x4AB0A7`) and `SaveNoOpenMapGame()` (`0x4AA9BF`) with `gSaveGames[gCurrentSaveGame]`, `EndingCutscene()` (`0x55B175`) the same; `CheckPointAreaEvent` (`0x48D0A2`, the record from `0x48CE81`–`0x48CE9A`) and `fastSkipEscapeExit()` (`0x564CCB`) with `gCheckpointSaves[gCurrentSaveGame]`; `LoadTeleportSave()` and the three `teleportTo…` (`0x562EEC`, `0x564E76`, `0x56502A`, `0x5651DE`) with `gTeleportSave`. `SaveGame` marks the slot's checkpoint record empty (`+0x935E` `0xFF`, `0x4AB000`–`0x4AB007`), fills `gSaveGames[slot]`, then writes the file, opened `r+b` or, failing that, `w+b` (`0x4AB21D`–`0x4AB281`): the word `0x3E8`, `gSaveGames[slot]` and `gCheckpointSaves[slot]`, `0x9408` bytes each, then, with the save's randomizer marker 1, the word `0x3E8` again and more (the section, by `save-format.md`'s layout; not traced here), the same on both paths (`0x4AB28A`–`0x4AB2FF`; `0x4AB385`–`0x4AB3FA`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19). **Matches**: a code the helper appends is in the live list `SaveGameData` copies, so it reaches the save's block (file `0x9333`) at the next save, and the checkpoint record at the next checkpoint, as a code the pickup appends does |
| MISSION COMPLETE (`memory.md`'s "a screen not identified") | `0x155DC7` and `0x155E13` in `0x154DA0` give 26 glyphs of a label the codes as frames, 13 to a row | the same function, MISSION COMPLETE; it reads the 26 codes and not the length | `PostGameStats()` `0x60924C` (routine entry 6, MISSION COMPLETE: "the Feats of Natu screen's note"): for i 0 to 12, `SWSetCurrentFrameIndex` of glyph 16 + i of panel 0's child 3 (the 45-glyph `   EQUIPMENT` line, `0x60968E`–`0x60969A`) to code i (`0x60A0E7`–`0x60A14F`) and glyph 31 + i to code 13 + i (`0x60A154`–`0x60A1A8`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19). **Matches** (26 codes, 13 to a row); `memory.md` does not say whether Windows reads the length. Reading all 26 shows what lies past the length; the helper writes only at the length, so it adds nothing there |
| a new game | not recorded | the list cleared: the slot's record cleared, then loaded | `StartNewGameExit()` `0x563363`: `ClearSaveGame(&gSaveGames[slot])` (`0x56337E`–`0x5633A2`), which zeroes the record's 26 codes and length (`0x4C25A5`–`0x4C25CD`, among its other stores; the function has no early return, its one `ret` at `0x4C2797`), marks the slot's checkpoint record empty (`0x56346E`–`0x563484`), fills the record's starting state (below), and `LoadGame` of that record (`0x563861`–`0x563868`), which copies the zeroed list and length. No instruction between `ClearSaveGame` and `LoadGame` writes the record's `+0x9331` to `+0x934B` | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19). **Not in `memory.md`**. A new game's list starts empty, and only the pickup appends |
| save states | not recorded here | the whole `gGameState`, list and length with it | `saveState()` deflates `gGameState`, `0x8CCCF8` bytes (`0x4AE4E5`–`0x4AE4F1`), and `loadState()` inflates it back (`0x4AF4EE`–`0x4AF4FD`): a state's load puts back the list and the length as they were, as it does the inventory (`gGameState+0x1DDDE`) | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19). **Not in `memory.md`'s list section**. A state's load takes back codes appended since, with the items behind them; the helper appends again for items still owned |
| every other reader and writer | every reference to `+0x3D0180` to `+0x3D019A` in `.text`: the pickup, the load, the save, `0x154DA0` | the same four | `grep` over `all.asm` for operands `<gGameState+0xae0>` to `<gGameState+0xaff>`: 11 instructions name the list or the length (`+0xAE0`: `0x4AC18F`, `0x561562`, `0x60A106`, `0x60A15F`, `0x64290A`; `+0xAFA`: `0x4AC1B0`, `0x561539`, `0x6428B9`, `0x6428FA`, `0x642914`, `0x64291E`), all in the four functions above; the others in the range are `+0xAFC` and `+0xAFE` (the continue point). The block is `gCheckpointSaves[n − 1]` while that record's `+0x935E` is not `0xFF`, else `gSaveGames[n − 1]` ("the file select's AP logo and disk counts") | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-19). **Matches** the glyphs and the length; the block, as that section says, is the checkpoint record while it is in use, which `SaveGame` marks empty and `CheckPointAreaEvent` fills with the live list, so the row shows the list as of the last save or checkpoint, the helper's codes with the game's |

| the inventory the helper reads (`inventory[item]`) | the player state's `+0x108E + ID` | the same | the Lookup's `inventory` row | measured (the Lookup) |

### What differs from Windows

- **Nothing `watch_pickups` reads or writes.** The pickup's rule (icons 0, `0x6E` and `0xE8` and
  items outside 3 to 26 and 32 to 44 left out, the append while the signed length is below 26),
  the icon at the record's `+0x08` in records of 100 bytes, 51 records in the installed `Objects`
  with the same icons, the list at `gGameState+0xAE0` and its length at `+0x1A`, the load and the
  save of both, and the start-with array at the section's `+0x689` in `randoItemKey`'s order all
  agree. No change to `pickups`, `record`, `rando::started_with` or `Game::watch_pickups` is
  needed for Linux.
- **Named on Linux:** the pickup is `playerCollideProc`, the save `SaveGameData`, the new game's
  reset `ClearSaveGame` with `StartNewGameExit`, the starting items' order `randoItemKey`; the
  screen `memory.md`'s list section calls "not identified" is MISSION COMPLETE, `PostGameStats`
  (as "the Feats of Natu screen's note" found for Windows' `0x154DA0`).
- **Not in `memory.md`:** a new game clears the list (the record cleared, then loaded); a save
  state's load puts back the list as it was; MISSION COMPLETE reads the 26 codes without the length;
  the file select's row is the checkpoint record's while that is in use, so a code appended after
  the last checkpoint (by the pickup or the helper) shows there once the game saves or reaches the
  next checkpoint (the Linux bullet in `native/README.md` says so; its Windows paragraph in "Grants"
  says "once the game saves"). Whether Windows' new game, save
  states and entry blocks do the same is not recorded.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The table above. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly and reads of the binary and the installed `Objects`, 2026-11-19), each row as its Status says; the file select's ITEMS row showing the received items after a save, run in the game, 2026-11-19 (run 1: "Seen in the running game, 2026-11-19") |
| Method | `memory.md`'s "The pickup record holds the major-item list" and `save-format.md`'s "Major items are a list in pickup order at 0x9333" matched against `all.asm`: `playerCollideProc`'s item branch, `LoadGame`, `SaveGameData` and its nine callers, `SaveGame`'s file writes, `PostGameStats`, `StartNewGameExit`, `ClearSaveGame`, `saveState`, `loadState` and `refreshGameFile`, read by hand at the addresses cited; a census by `grep` over `all.asm` of the operands `<gGameState+0xae0>` to `<gGameState+0xaff>`, of the displacements `0xae0` to `0xafa`, of the record offsets `+0x9331` to `+0x934b`, of `gCurrentRando+0x689` to `+0x6a3` and of `SaveGameData`'s calls, each instruction's function by the nearest symbol above it; `randoItemKey` read from the binary's `LOAD` segment and the installed `Files/Objects` read through `tools/protected.py`'s `game_dir()`, by scratch Python scripts (not kept) |
| Build | B1 (Linux: `lab/linux-b1/Zexion`, SHA-256 `cd64cd9ec469…`), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it, and `native/src/game_layout.h` and `game_calls.cpp` as of 2026-11-19 |

## The save record is the file less its first two bytes

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | `LoadGame(SaveGameRec*)` (`0x560EE5`) reads the record at the file's offsets less 2, as the Windows build's memory holds a slot's block (`memory.md`, "Save data lives in Zexion.exe's static data"): it tests the word at record `+0x9400` for 1 (the file's randomizer marker, `0x9402`), reads the ITEMS counter at record `+0x9358` (file `0x935A`), the continue point at `+0x935A` and `+0x935C` (file `0x935C`, `0x935E`), 26 major-item bytes from `+0x9331` (file `0x9333`), 64 inventory bytes from `+0x92F1` (file `0x92F3`) and the map grid from `+0x877D` (file `0x877F`), each a field `save-format.md` records at that file offset. A `SaveGameRec` is `0x9408` bytes: `gSaveGames` (30 of them), `gCheckpointSaves` (30) and `gTeleportSave` (one) are its multiples. |
| Status | measured (by disassembly, 2026-11-18) |
| Method | `LoadGame`'s listing in `all.asm`, its loads from the record (`rdi`, kept at `[rbp-0x38]`) read on screen against `save-format.md`'s lookup table; the arrays' sizes from `nm -S` |
| Build | B1 (Linux) |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, `LoadGame(SaveGameRec*)` from `0x560EE5`, 2026-11-18. |

## `gGameState` has the Windows layout

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | `gGameState` (`0x9E7840`, `0x8CCCF8` bytes, in `.bss`) holds the state the Windows build keeps from `+0x3CF6A0` of its image, offset for offset, for every field checked: first four, by `LoadGame`'s stores, then many more (below). `LoadGame` stores the save's ITEMS counter at `gGameState+0xAC8`, its major-item list at `+0xAE0`, its randomizer marker at `+0x13738` and its map grid at `+0x14FFD`; the Windows build keeps the same four at `+0x3D0168` (`pickup_counter`), `+0x3D0180` (`pickup_list`), `+0x3E2DD8` (`rando_marker`) and `+0x3E469D` (`map_grid`), and each Windows address less its Linux offset is `0x3CF6A0`. So for any Windows target inside that range, the Linux address is assumed to be the Windows address less `0x3CF6A0` plus `0x9E7840`, until each is checked (the Lookup's "shift"). |
| | **Where it might not hold.** A field whose type is another size on Linux would move everything after it: `wchar_t` is 4 bytes on Linux and 2 on Windows, and `long` is 8 and 4. The first four agreeing fields spanned `+0xAC8` to `+0x14FFD`; the fields confirmed since (below) reach `+0x8C6B48`. The translation tables, which hold `wchar_t`, are not in `gGameState`: `gCurrentTranslation` is a global of its own, with 4-byte characters ("Mapped by disassembly: translations"). |
| | **Confirmed further, by disassembly** (the first four "Mapped by disassembly" sections): the shift also gives the message slots (`gGameState+0x8C6B48`), the item pool (`+0x79E990`) and the object pool (`+0x388DA0`), each reached as `gGameState` plus a displacement, so `objdump` names no symbol for them; the death count (`+0xAD4`), the color slot table (`+0x137FF`), the per-cell map byte at `+0x15B69`, the message counter (`+0x1CD4C`) and cursor (`+0x8CC3E8`), `last_message`, `frame_clock`, `fade_level`, `ailments` and `flags`; and `fade` with its saved copy (`+0x13448`, `+0x13598`: "Mapped by disassembly: the RETURN HOME prompt"). So `gGameState` holds no field of another size on Linux up to at least `+0x8C6B48`. |
| | **The player state.** `LoadGame` copies the 64 inventory bytes to `*gMainCharacterSpriteP + 0x108E + ID`, the offset `memory.md` records for the Windows player state ("Live inventory: writing an item's byte grants it", the game reading `0x108E + ID`). The Windows player state is at `+0x3EC3F0`, which would be `gGameState+0x1CD50` (`0xA04590`) here; the pointer read that address in the game, at the title screen and in play ("Read from the running game"). |
| Status | measured (the four offsets, by disassembly, 2026-11-18; the further fields above, by disassembly, the same day; `save_loaded`, the pickup counter, the randomizer marker and the player state's place read in the game: "Read from the running game"); assumed (fields of the range not named here, by the shift) |
| Method | `LoadGame`'s stores in `all.asm` read on screen, against `memory.md`'s Lookup table; the reference counts by `grep -c '<gGameState+0x...>'` over `all.asm` |
| Build | B1 (Linux), against B1 (Windows) as `memory.md` records it |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-18: `mov WORD PTR [rip+...],ax # 9e8308 <gGameState+0xac8>` after `movzx eax,WORD PTR [rax+0x9358]`; the list loop to `<gGameState+0xae0>` from `[rdx+rax*1+0x9331]`; `# 9faf78 <gGameState+0x13738>` after `[rax+0x9400]`; the grid loop to `<gGameState+0x14ffd>` from `+0x877d`. |

## Read from the running game

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | **At the title screen**, no save loaded since the game started, read through the Linux helper's `read` at the addresses the Lookup gives (each from the binary's symbol table): `save_loaded` (`gGameState+0xACA`) `0xFF`, the value `memory.md` gives for no save loaded ("Telling play from menus, the title screen and death", where the start-up value was not measured on Windows); the pickup counter (`gGameState+0xAC8`) 0, the randomizer marker (`gGameState+0x13738`) 0 and `gCurrentSaveGame` 0; `gMainCharacterSpriteP` `0xA04590`, which is `gGameState+0x1CD50`, the Windows player state's place by the shift ("`gGameState` has the Windows layout", "The player state"). The first 4,096 bytes of `.rodata` (`0x68C000`) equal the binary's: the binary runs at its link addresses (`builds.md`, "B1 on Linux"). |
| | **In play**, in slot 4 (a randomizer save) with the game running from start-up: `save_loaded` 0 (a save loaded); `gCurrentSaveGame` 3, so 0-based, as `memory.md` records `current_slot`; the randomizer marker 1 and the pickup counter 95, as `Save Game 4.sav` holds them (`0x9402` = 1, `0x935A` = `0x5F`; slot 3's counter is `0x8B`); `gMainCharacterSpriteP` still `0xA04590`. |
| | **The process.** Found by its name, `Zexion`, and identified as B1 by the binary it runs (`/proc/<pid>/exe`: build id and hash); under Flatpak's Steam it had a pid of its own namespace (host pid 33866, its own 873; in an earlier launch 33353 and 763), shared the client's network namespace, and saw the game folder at the host's path. The launch options' `LD_PRELOAD` reached it with the helper first and Steam's overlay after it, and the helper loaded and served (`native/README.md`, "The Linux helper"). |
| Status | measured (in the game, 2026-11-18) |
| Method | `tools/helper.py status`; a script reading the values above through `client/linux_helper.py`; `/proc/<pid>/status` (`NSpid`), `/proc/<pid>/ns/net`, `/proc/<pid>/environ`, `/proc/<pid>/maps` and `/proc/<pid>/root` |
| Build | B1 (Linux) |
| Evidence | Output read on screen, 2026-11-18, the game at its title screen and then in play in slot 4, started from Flatpak Steam on the test PC, the helper the branch built that day (`native/build/libzexion_ap.so`: 647b3a2's, then with `callers`, as committed in 6dfc54a) copied into the game's folder. A first launch, with the file not yet copied, had the preload in its environment and no helper mapped. |

the client's lines said, as `native/README.md` ("The Linux helper") records it part by part; no

ARCHIPELAGO on the game-mode panel, and the seed's new game started from it in an empty slot (the client: "The new game holds the AP seed (applied slot 5)"). A received Energy Tank's and Missile Container's popups with their items, first popping in and out whole (the helper passed 0 for `createGameMessage`'s 5th and 6th arguments), then opening and closing as the game's own once it passed 100 and 1. This world's own two disks by the pod counted at their pickup, with the client attached. A death RunB sent, dealt in the game; the owner's own death reaching RunB, not dealt back. The map's cursor. `seen_marker_code`'s redirect. `text_cut_code`'s mod-9 branch. ## The per-frame call: SDL_GL_SwapWindow through the PLT

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | The binary imports SDL2's `SDL_GL_SwapWindow` by name (undefined in `.dynsym`) and calls it only through its PLT entry (`0x40C120`, a jump through the GOT slot `0x6BB700`), from two places: `SWHardwareEnd(SpriteWorldRec*)` (`0x4CAF9A`) and `UpdatePalette()` (`0x652552`). So a preloaded library that defines `SDL_GL_SwapWindow` is called in SDL's place from both (the dynamic linker binds the slot to the first definition, the preloaded one's): the Linux helper's frame call (`native/README.md`, "The Linux helper"). `SWHardwareEnd` itself is called from four places (all.asm, 2026-11-18): `updateGame()` (`0x670077`), `advanceFrameAndUpdateSpriteWorld()` (`0x670FF6`), `UpdateKeys()` (`0x4E6B07`) and `loadState()` (`0x4B35F8`), so the helper's frame work also runs at a swap inside a `loadState`; which load that is, and what state the game is in at that swap, is not traced. **A known limit of the helper:** it takes a call by the one return address it has (inside `SWHardwareEnd`, the same whoever called it), so it can't tell a `loadState`'s swap; a grant that lands there can be overwritten by the load's copy of the inventory with its popup already out, and the next poll gives the item again (nothing is lost, the popup is the cost). Telling them apart needs `loadState`'s own state or the caller's return address from the stack (the game has no frame pointers), neither traced (review of the port, 2026-11-19). |
| | **In the game** (2026-11-18, the test PC's 165 Hz display): the helper counted 165 calls a second, one a display refresh, so the swap probably waits for the refresh (vsync; inferred from the rate, not measured), and the rate follows the player's display: the helper's frame work must not assume a rate. **By call site** (the helper's `callers`, each return address named by the function holding it): at the title screen and in play every call of the second came from `SWHardwareEnd` (return address `0x4CAF9F`), 165 a second; `UpdatePalette`'s (`0x652557`) was called once in the whole run, at start-up, and not again. `UpdatePalette` swaps `gPaletteWindowP`, not the game's window (its argument, by disassembly); it is called only from `ProcessPalette()`, and `SWHardwareEnd` from `UpdateKeys()` and `loadState()`. So the game's window is swapped once a frame, from `SWHardwareEnd`; frame work must take that site's calls alone. |
| Status | measured (the import and the two call sites, by disassembly, 2026-11-18; the binding to the preloaded definition, the call rate, and the calls by site, in the game, the same day: at the title screen and in play; not seen in other menus, a death or the ending) |
| Method | `nm -D` on the copy; `grep 'SDL_GL_SwapWindow' all.asm`, each call's function by the nearest symbol above it, and the callers of those functions; `tools/helper.py status` in the game |
| Build | B1 (Linux) |
| Evidence | `lab/linux-b1/all.asm`, 2026-11-18: `call 40c120 <SDL_GL_SwapWindow@plt>` at lines for `0x4caf9a` and `0x652552`, and no other reference. |

## Reading the running game

| Field | |
|---|---|
| Value | **The client can't read the game itself.** Reading another process's memory on Linux (`/proc/<pid>/mem`, `process_vm_readv`) takes the kernel's ptrace permission. With Yama's `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope` at 1, the default of many distributions, a process may read only its own descendants, and the game, started by Steam, is not the client's. The test PC has the scope at 1: there a process that was not a stand-in game's ancestor was refused its `/proc/<pid>/mem` (`EACCES` on opening it). So the Linux helper reads the game's memory for the client, from inside it (`read`: `native/README.md`, "The Linux helper"), and opens no way to it through the kernel to another process, whatever the scope. Its socket does serve `read` and `write` to a connection, so a connection must show the helper's token first (below). |
| | **Superseded: opening ptrace while a client is connected.** The helper's first design called `prctl(PR_SET_PTRACER, PR_SET_PTRACER_ANY)` while a client was connected and `PR_SET_PTRACER` 0 when it left. With the stand-in game, a process not its ancestor was refused its memory before a client connected, read it while one was, and was refused again after. It was replaced the same day, after review: the window let every process of the user in, not just the client, and by the kernel's rules (assumed: `/proc/<pid>/mem`'s permission is checked when it is opened, and an attach outlives the window) what one opened in the window stayed open after it. |
| | **Pids.** Flatpak's Steam runs the game in its own process-id namespace, so that its pid inside differs from the one in the host's `/proc` (both are in `/proc/<pid>/status`, `NSpid`): seen with the game ("Read from the running game"). |
| | **The socket's token.** The socket is abstract, so any process of the user in the network namespace can connect to it, and the game under Flatpak's Steam shared the client's network namespace ("Read from the running game"): a Flatpak app with network access is such a process, and it can't ptrace the game or read the Steam app's folder. The uid check (`SO_PEERCRED`) is no defense against it. Through `read` the helper gives any game memory (the heap's, libsteam_api's), through `write` its `.bss`, which holds pointers the game calls through: `gExitActionP` (`updateGame` calls it), `gExitActions`, `gMoveProcs`, `gAreaEventProcs`, `gSpriteDrawProcs`, `gEnemyActionProcs` (`0x12F8060`, called by `executeEnemyAction`), and each sprite record's move procedure at `+0x168` in the pools of `gGameState` (found by the reviews of the port, 2026-11-19, by reading the code and this file's rows, not run). So the helper writes a token (32 kernel random bytes, hex) into `ZexionAP/token` of its data folder when the game starts (mode 0600, the folder 0700) and serves a connection only after `auth <token>`; the client finds it through `/proc/<pid>/root` and the game's environment (the sandbox's folder under Flatpak's Steam), or in the host's own folders, tries each it finds (the newest file first, a connection each), and sends it only to a process it has checked is the game. `write` takes only the ranges the client writes (`client_write_ranges`: the inventory bytes 0 to 49 and the six supply numbers of the player's record, the map cells' item states, the map grid), the rest of the `.bss` refused: a list of the pointers to refuse could not be known whole. The player's record is a sprite record, so its supply and inventory fields are open; nothing shows they hold a pointer. What the owner must check in the game on Linux: with native Steam and with Flatpak Steam, the client attaches (the helper's stderr line `serving build B1`, no `no token`; `tools/helper.py status` prints the game, the helper and `memory: readable`); `ls -l` of the folder holding `token` (the file `-rw-------`, the folder `drwx------`); with another connection (`python3 -c` with a socket to `\0zexion-ap-<inner pid>` sending `read 0x400000 16`) refused with `error auth required`. |
| Status | measured (the test PC's ptrace scope, and the refusal, with the stand-in game, 2026-11-18; the pid namespace, with the game, the same day); superseded (the first design's window, measured with the stand-in game the same day); assumed (the kernel's rules behind the window's flaw); the token: stand-in tested (handshake, refusals, modes, the client's reading through `/proc/<pid>/root`: Ubuntu 24.04, GCC 13, 2026-11-19), not run in the game: that `/proc/<pid>/root` and the game's `XDG_DATA_HOME` reach the folder under Flatpak's Steam and native Steam is assumed |
| Method | `cat /proc/sys/kernel/yama/ptrace_scope`; `tests/test_native_linux.py`, `test_the_games_memory_is_never_opened_to_other_processes` (and, for the first design, the test it replaced, `test_memory_is_readable_only_while_a_client_is_connected`), `test_the_helper_serves_nothing_before_the_token`, `test_write_is_refused_outside_what_the_client_writes`, `test_a_stale_token_found_first_does_not_stop_the_live_one`, `test_an_unauthenticated_connection_cannot_hold_the_helper_past_its_deadline`, `test_a_connection_that_sets_something_has_it_put_back_when_it_goes`; `tests/test_linux_helper.py`'s token tests; a by-hand read of `/proc/<pid>/mem` from a new session (`setsid`), which printed `errno 13 Permission denied` |
| Build | any (the stand-in game, not Zexion) |
| Evidence | The scope read `1`, 2026-11-18; the tests passed on the test PC the same day. |
